8.3 Polynomial Ring R_q State Model

Poly states and legal transitions:
  [Allocated] ──sample/decode──► [Normal]
  [Normal]    ──forward_ntt──►   [Ntt]
  [Ntt]       ──inverse_ntt──►   [Normal]
  [Ntt]       ──pointwise_mul──► [Ntt]      (two Ntt-tagged polys only)
  [Normal]    ──compress──►      [Compressed bytes]
  [Normal]    ──encode──►        [Serialized bytes]
  [Serialized]──decode──►        [Normal]
  [Normal/Ntt]──zeroize──►       [Freed]

Invariant: the Domain tag is set at construction and flipped only by forward_ntt/inverse_ntt. Any function accepting two Poly arguments for multiplication must statically or dynamically assert both are Domain::Ntt.

8.4 ML-KEM Lifecycle State Machine

States: Unkeyed → Keyed → Encapsulated → Decapsulated

Unkeyed ──keygen(seed)──────────────────────────────► Keyed
          (A=ExpandA(rho), t=A*s+e, pk=(rho,t1), sk=...)

Keyed ──encaps(pk, m)──────────────────────────────► Encapsulated
        ((K,r) = G(m, H(pk)),  c = CPA_Enc(pk, m; r))

Encapsulated ──decaps(sk, c)────────────────────────► Decapsulated
               (m' = CPA_Dec(sk,c), re-encrypt c''=CPA_Enc(pk,m';r'),
                compare ct_eq(c,c'') → K or PRF(z,c))

Transitions failing ct_eq still return SharedSecret; never return an error or abort. Any path returning a distinguishable error breaks CCA2.

Sequence: Encaps/Decaps (happy path)

Alice (has pk)                     Bob (has sk, pk)
  m ←← random 32 B
  (K, r) = G(m, H(pk))
  c = CPA_Enc(pk, m; r)
  ─────── (K, c) ───────────────►
                                   m' = CPA_Dec(sk, c)
                                   r' = G(m', H(pk)).1
                                   c'' = CPA_Enc(pk, m'; r')
                                   K_out = ct_select(ct_eq(c,c''), K, PRF(z,c))
                                   ◄──── K_out ──────
  assert K == K_out   (honest)

Sequence: Decaps with tampered ciphertext (failure path)

Attacker sends c_bad ≠ c
  Bob:  m' = CPA_Dec(sk, c_bad)   (garbage plaintext)
        c'' = CPA_Enc(pk, m')      (re-encrypt → c'' ≠ c_bad with overwhelming prob)
        ct_eq(c_bad, c'') == 0
        K_out = PRF(z, c_bad)      (deterministic pseudo-random; not K)
  No timing difference between this path and the honest path (P1, P3, I4).

8.5 ML-DSA Signing State Machine

States: Unkeyed → Keyed → Signing (loop) → Signed

Unkeyed ──keygen(seed)──────────────────────────────► Keyed
          (A=ExpandA(rho), s1~CBD, s2~CBD,
           t=A*s1+s2, split(t) → t1,t0)

Keyed ──sign(sk, msg, rng)──────────────────────────► Signing (loop)

Signing (loop):
  y ←← uniform in (-gamma1, gamma1)^l   [masking vector]
  w = A*y
  w1 = HighBits(w, 2*gamma2)
  c_tilde = H(tr || msg || w1)
  c = SampleInBall(c_tilde)             [sparse ±1 challenge]
  z = y + c*s1
  r0 = LowBits(w - c*s2, 2*gamma2)
  IF ||z||_inf >= gamma1 - beta  → REJECT, resample y
  IF ||r0||_inf >= gamma2 - beta → REJECT, resample y
  h = MakeHint(-c*t0, w - c*s2 + c*t0, 2*gamma2)
  IF ||c*t0||_inf >= gamma2 → REJECT, resample y
  IF popcount(h) > omega → REJECT, resample y
  output (c_tilde, z, h)  ──────────────────────────► Signed

Expected iterations per sign: ~4 (ML-DSA-44), ~5 (ML-DSA-65), ~7 (ML-DSA-87). Rejection sampling is security-critical: I7, P4.

Sequence: Verification (happy path)

Verifier has (pk=(rho,t1), msg, sig=(c_tilde,z,h)):
  A = ExpandA(rho)
  c = SampleInBall(c_tilde)
  w1' = UseHint(h, A*z - c*t1*2^d, 2*gamma2)
  c_tilde' = H(tr || msg || w1')
  accept iff:
    c_tilde' == c_tilde      (challenge match)
    ||z||_inf < gamma1-beta  (z in bounds)
    popcount(h) <= omega      (hint count bounded)

Sequence: Verification (tampered signature — failure path)

Attacker flips a bit in z → ||z||_inf may exceed gamma1-beta → reject (I10)
Attacker flips c_tilde → c_tilde' ≠ c_tilde → reject
Attacker forges h with > omega 1-bits → reject on hint count
All rejections: deterministic, no branch on secret data.

8.6 NTT Domain Transition and Arithmetic Model

Twiddle factors (zetas) for ML-KEM (q=3329):

  • Primitive 512th root of unity: zeta = 17 mod 3329.
  • Precomputed: ZETAS[i] = Montgomery(zeta^(BitRev7(i))) for i=0..127.
  • Forward NTT: Cooley-Tukey butterfly, 7 layers, in-place on [i32; 256].
  • Inverse NTT: Gentleman-Sande butterfly, 7 layers, multiply by n^{-1} = 3303 mod 3329 at end.

Butterfly operations (signatures only, no bodies):

fn ct_butterfly(a: i32, b: i32, zeta: i32, q: i32) -> (i32, i32);
fn gs_butterfly(a: i32, b: i32, zeta: i32, q: i32) -> (i32, i32);
fn pointwise_mul_ntt(a: &PolyNtt, b: &PolyNtt) -> PolyNtt;
fn ntt(p: Poly<Normal>) -> Poly<Ntt>;
fn inv_ntt(p: Poly<Ntt>) -> Poly<Normal>;

Invariant I5: inv_ntt(ntt(p)) == p (coefficient-wise mod q). Verified by round-trip property test over 10,000 random polynomials and by diff-test against schoolbook.

NTT-based matrix-vector product (shape conventions):

ML-KEM keygen: t = A*s + e
  A: [K][K] polys (Ntt), s: [K] polys (Ntt), e: [K] polys (Normal→Ntt)
  t[i] = sum_j( A[i][j] * s[j] ) + e[i],  all in NTT; inv_ntt per component

ML-KEM encrypt: u = A^T * r + e1
  A^T[j][i] = A[i][j];  pointwise NTT mul, accumulate, inv_ntt, add e1

ML-DSA: w = A * y (K×L matrix times L-vec)
  w[i] = sum_j( A[i][j] * y_ntt[j] ); inv_ntt → LowBits/HighBits in Normal domain

8.7 Serialization / Wire Formats

ML-KEM byte layouts (per FIPS 203, §5):

Field Encoding Bits/coeff Bytes (K=2/3/4)
rho raw 32 B — 32
t (public key NTT-domain polys) 12 bits/coeff 12 384·K
s (secret key; eta1 bits) CBD packed eta1 varies
u (ciphertext vector) Compress(du bits) du 32·du·K
v (ciphertext scalar) Compress(dv bits) dv 32·dv

Compress(x, d) = round(x · 2^d / q) mod 2^d — lossy. Decompress(y, d) = round(y · q / 2^d). Error bound per FIPS 203 Appendix A.

ML-DSA byte layouts (per FIPS 204, §5):

Field Encoding Bits/coeff Notes
rho 32 B raw — public seed
t1 10 bits/coeff 10 HighBits output; per K polys
t0 13 bits/coeff signed 13 secret; ±2^{d-1} range
s1/s2 eta+1 bits (eta=2→3b, eta=4→4b) eta+1 secret; small coeffs
z 18/20 bits/coeff 18 or 20 gamma1=2^17 or 2^19
h omega + k bytes 1 b/coeff packed hint bitmap
c_tilde 32/48/64 B — lambda/4 bytes; challenge seed

SHAKE-based seed expansion (per-function labels):

Symbol Hash fn Input Output
G SHA3-512 32 B seed (rho 32 B, sigma 32 B)
H SHA3-256 pk bytes 32 B
PRF SHAKE256 sigma
XOF SHAKE128 rho
KDF (sign) SHAKE256 K

8.8 Reduction and Modular Arithmetic Model

Montgomery reduction (q=3329, R=2^16):

Step Operation Range
Input a*b as i64 [0, q²·R)
m = (a*b mod R) * q_inv mod R lower 16 bits [0, R)
t = (a*b + m*q) >> 16 shift right by log2(R) (-q, q)
t += q if t < 0 conditional add [0, q)

Barrett reduction (q=3329): t = a - q·floor(a·k/2^26) with k=80635; range [0, 2q) → subtract q if t>=q.

For q=8380417 (ML-DSA): Montgomery with R=2^23; similar structure, larger shift. All intermediates use i64 to avoid overflow.

Centered binomial sampling (CBD_eta):

CBD_eta(b): read 2*eta bits = a[0..eta-1] XOR b[0..eta-1]; coeff = sum(a) - sum(b)
Range: [-eta, eta]; distributed as Binomial(2*eta, 1/2) - eta
Constant-time: no secret-dependent branches; XOR of uniform bits.

Rejection sampling for uniform mod q:

Read 3 bytes → 24 bits → two 12-bit candidates d1, d2 from SHAKE128 stream
Accept d if d < q (3329); else discard and continue
Variable-time (public matrix A only — P7); expected ~1.74 bytes per coeff.

8.9 Bit-packing and Encoding Edge Cases

Edge Case Expected Behavior
Decode poly with coeff ≥ q Reject (I10); return Err(MalformedInput)
Ciphertext wrong byte length Reject immediately before any crypto op
Signature z coeff out of (-gamma1, gamma1) verify returns false; no panic
Hint with > omega 1-bits verify returns false
c_tilde length mismatch for param set verify returns false
Signature byte count ≠ param-set sig_bytes verify returns false immediately
encode(decode(x)) != x Signals internal consistency error; invariant I9
Compress/Decompress round-trip error Bounded by FIPS 203 Appendix A; not an error
All-zero secret key bytes (degenerate) Valid input; KAT must still match
rho of all 0xFF Valid; ExpandA still runs; no special-case

8.10 Worked Example: ML-KEM-512 Encapsulation (Conceptual)

Input: pk = (rho, t1_bytes); m = 0x00...00 (32 B)

1. H(pk) → 32 B hash of public key bytes
2. (K, r) = G(m || H(pk)) → K=first 32 B, r=next 32 B
3. A = ExpandA(rho) using XOF(rho || (i,j)) — k=2, 4 polys in Ntt domain
4. r_vec = Sample_poly_CBD(r, eta1=3) — 2 polys, Normal domain
5. e1_vec = Sample_poly_CBD(sigma, eta2=2) — 2 polys noise
6. e2_poly = Sample_poly_CBD(sigma, eta2=2) — 1 poly noise
7. r_ntt = ntt(r_vec)
8. u = inv_ntt(A^T * r_ntt) + e1   — Compress(u, du=10) → 320 B
9. m_poly = Decompress(m, 1)        — 1-bit per coeff poly
10. v = inv_ntt(t1_ntt * r_ntt) + e2 + m_poly  — Compress(v, dv=4) → 128 B
11. c = encode(u) || encode(v)       — 448 B ciphertext
12. output (K, c)

Malformed-input example:

Input: c with encode(v) truncated by 1 byte
  → byte-length check in decode fails before any poly operation
  → return Err(MalformedCiphertext) (I10)
  → no information leakage; same timing as a normal-length invalid ciphertext

8.11 Constant-Time Execution Model

Operation CT requirement Mechanism
FO ciphertext comparison Must not branch on result ct_eq returns u8 mask; ct_select picks K or PRF(z,c)
CBD sampling No secret-dependent branch XOR + popcount on uniform bits; no early exit
Rejection sampling (public A) Variable-time allowed P7; A is not secret
Rejection sampling (sign loop iteration count) Variable-time allowed loop count is not secret (leaks only statistical info)
z, r0, hint bounds check during signing Values are secret ct_cmp for bounds; result fed into loop-continue flag without branching on value
zeroize on secret structs Volatile write barrier zeroize crate or equivalent; must not be optimized out
Memory indexing on secret keys Must be constant stride No sk[secret_idx]; all accesses via fixed-size loops

Secret data categories: s1, s2, t0, y (masking), m (message in KEM), K (shared secret before output), z intermediate, r in encaps.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
ADR-01 Implementation language Rust (2021 edition) C, C++, Go, Python, Zig Memory safety without GC; zero-cost abstractions; zeroize, subtle ecosystem; no undefined behavior on integer overflow in debug mode C wins for embedded HSM targets where no Rust toolchain exists or where a certified C compiler (e.g., IAR) is mandatory for safety certification
ADR-02 Build system Cargo (workspace) Bazel, CMake, Meson Standard Rust toolchain; workspace unifies all pqc-* crates; reproducible via Cargo.lock Bazel wins in a multi-language monorepo (Rust + C + Proto + Go) where hermetic cross-language build caching across teams is the primary driver
ADR-03 Dependency policy Minimal: zeroize, subtle, sha3 (RustCrypto) optional feature-gated; no rand in core ring, openssl, boringssl Audit surface; sha3 may be replaced by pqc-hash; subtle for CT primitives; zeroize for secret erasure ring or openssl wins when FIPS 140-3 certified cryptographic module boundaries are required by a customer contract; the from-scratch crate has no such certification
ADR-04 Constant-time primitives subtle crate (Choice, CtOption, ConditionallySelectable) Manual volatile_read, inline asm subtle is audited; compiler-barrier patterns; integrates with zeroize; no secret-dependent branches Inline assembly wins on deeply embedded targets where LLVM cannot be trusted to preserve barriers, or when formal hardware CT proof is required (e.g., CHERI-based isolation)
ADR-05 Zeroization zeroize crate (Zeroize + ZeroizeOnDrop derive) Manual memset, SecretBox Prevents compiler elision; ZeroizeOnDrop on all secret key structs; covers heap and stack Manual memset_s (C11) or platform-specific SecureZeroMemory (Windows) wins when interfacing with a C ABI layer that owns the allocation; the zeroize crate cannot traverse opaque FFI pointers
ADR-06 SHA-3 / SHAKE pqc-hash (from-scratch Keccak-f[1600]) cross-ref #88 sha3 crate, openssl Spec compliance; byte-exact ACVP verification; full control over absorb/squeeze interface; reuse with project #88 The sha3 RustCrypto crate wins in a production library that must audit a single well-maintained dependency rather than own a Keccak implementation; applies when cryptographic engineering headcount is limited
ADR-07 Serialization format FIPS 203/204 bit-packed binary (custom pqc-encode) serde + JSON/CBOR, bincode No generic serializer; exact FIPS byte layout required; serde adds unnecessary abstraction and cannot express d-bit packing serde+JSON wins in a REST API context where key material is transported as Base64-encoded JSON and human-readability of intermediate representations is required for debugging by non-specialist operators
ADR-08 Integer storage for Zq i32 (signed, 32-bit) with i64 intermediates u16, u32, i64 throughout i32 holds both q=3329 (fits u12) and q=8380417 (fits i24); intermediate products need i64; Barrett/Montgomery in i64, store as i32 u16 throughout wins in a vectorized SIMD NTT (AVX2 SIMD packs 16×u16); the narrower type doubles parallelism in the butterfly loop, at cost of requiring ML-KEM-only (q=3329, fits 16-bit) and a separate ML-DSA path
ADR-09 NTT twiddle factor precomputation Static const arrays of Montgomery-encoded zetas Runtime computation, lazy static Eliminates runtime init; compile-time const eval; no mutex/once overhead; fully inlined Runtime lazy-static computation wins on a target where ROM is severely constrained (< 4 KB) and the zeta table must be in RAM; embedded MCU with bank-switched flash is the canonical case
ADR-10 Polynomial domain safety Domain enum tag on Poly + runtime assert (debug) / type-level in release Separate PolyNtt type, no enforcement Runtime assert in debug catches misuse; type-level requires const generics (Rust stable support marginal); domain tag overhead: 1 byte Separate PolyNtt type wins when the codebase targets a pre-stable Rust compiler with limited const-generics support, or when the type discipline is enforced at a higher-level API boundary by a trusted wrapper
ADR-11 Randomness (keygen/sign) Caller-supplied &mut impl RngCore or deterministic seed for KAT OS-rng in core, getrandom Core crates are no-std compatible; KAT reproducibility requires deterministic hooks; caller injects OsRng in CLI Embedding getrandom directly wins for a higher-level library (not a primitive crate) targeting WASM or a TEE environment where the caller cannot easily supply an RNG handle; convenience outweighs control
ADR-12 Crate organization Workspace of 11 focused crates Monolith, 2–3 crates Fine-grained auditing; each crate has single responsibility; pqc-kat depends on all, keeps test infra separate A 2-crate split (pqc-crypto + pqc-cli) wins when publish friction (11 separate crates.io packages, version lockstep) is a higher burden than audit granularity, e.g., for an internal-only library
ADR-13 KAT test vectors FIPS 203/204 ACVP JSON files, checked into pqc-kat/vectors/ NIST PQC submission vectors (pre-FIPS), third-party vectors ACVP vectors are normative for FIPS compliance; pre-FIPS Kyber vectors differ in domain separator; checked-in avoids network dependency Network-fetched ACVP vectors win when the codebase must track fast-moving FIPS errata in real time without a manual update cycle; applicable in a NIST ACVP automated submission pipeline
ADR-14 Property-based testing proptest quickcheck, manual fuzz proptest shrinking; macro-based strategy; no_std not required for test crates quickcheck wins in a team already using it for other subsystems where sharing shrinking strategies and custom Arbitrary impls across the codebase is more valuable than proptest's macro ergonomics
ADR-15 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) + afl.rs Honggfuzz, custom harness Dual fuzzer: libFuzzer for coverage-guided, AFL for mutation; targets: fuzz_decaps, fuzz_verify, fuzz_decode_poly Honggfuzz wins in a CI environment where persistent-mode fuzzing across process restarts is unreliable; Honggfuzz's fork-based model handles target crashes more robustly
ADR-16 Benchmarking criterion (statistical, HTML reports) iai (instruction counts), std::time Criterion provides warm-up, outlier detection, regression tracking; add iai later for CI instruction-count gates iai (instruction counts via Valgrind/Callgrind) wins in a CI environment where wall-clock timing is noisy due to shared runners; instruction counts are deterministic and do not require a dedicated benchmark machine
ADR-17 Logging tracing crate (structured spans) log crate, slog, println Span-based; zero-cost when disabled; KAT and CLI use it; no secret values in trace fields (P10) The log crate wins for an embedded target where tracing's macro machinery adds unacceptable binary size overhead (> 50 KB); log with a minimal backend is the embedded standard
ADR-18 Metrics / telemetry metrics facade + metrics-exporter-prometheus in CLI OpenTelemetry SDK, StatsD Lightweight; rejection-loop counters, keygen/encaps/decaps/sign/verify op counts; never record secret-dependent values OpenTelemetry SDK wins when the organization has standardized on OTLP and requires trace-metric correlation (exemplars linking a slow decaps span to its rejection-count metric)
ADR-19 CI/CD GitHub Actions: cargo test, cargo clippy, cargo fuzz (short run), KAT suite CircleCI, Jenkins, self-hosted Standard Rust CI; matrix: stable + beta + nightly; MSRV pin in Cargo.toml; deny list via cargo-deny Self-hosted Jenkins wins for an air-gapped environment (classified / regulated) where no third-party CI infrastructure may touch the source tree
ADR-20 Reproducible builds Cargo.lock committed; pinned toolchain via rust-toolchain.toml Floating toolchain, no lockfile Ensures bit-identical KAT outputs across environments; lockfile in workspace root A floating toolchain wins in a library crate (not a binary) intended for wide downstream consumption, where locking the toolchain imposes version constraints on dependents who may need a newer Rust edition
ADR-21 Dependency audit cargo-deny (license + advisory) in CI cargo-audit alone cargo-deny checks licenses (MIT/Apache-2.0 only), known CVEs, duplicate versions; blocks merge on violation cargo-audit alone wins when the team has a separate license-compliance process and cargo-deny's policy DSL is too rigid for a multi-team monorepo with heterogeneous license requirements
ADR-22 no_std support pqc-core, pqc-poly, pqc-ntt, pqc-hash, pqc-sample, pqc-encode, pqc-ct are no_std std throughout Embedded targets (HSM, TEE); pqc-kem, pqc-sign are no_std + alloc optional; CLI requires std std throughout wins for a pure server-side deployment where embedded or TEE targets are explicitly out of scope and no_std complexity adds maintenance overhead without a present customer need
ADR-23 Side-channel review tooling dudect statistical timing test + manual review via cargo-asm Formal CT verification (ct-verif), binsec dudect statistical: detects timing differences in CT paths; cargo-asm audits codegen; formal tools not in Rust ecosystem Formal CT verification (binsec or ct-verif) wins when a government customer contract requires a formal proof of constant-time execution rather than a statistical bound; applicable to FIPS 140-3 level 3+ evaluations
ADR-24 Packaging / distribution crates.io publish per crate; pqc-cli as binary in cargo install Docker-only, vendored Standard Rust distribution; Docker image for KAT/fuzz runs; pqc-cli installable via cargo Docker-only distribution wins for a deployment-centric team where end-users are Ops engineers, not Rust developers, and the Docker container is the standard unit of deployment
ADR-25 MSRV Rust 1.82 (stable) nightly, 1.70 1.82 stabilizes const generics subset needed; subtle and zeroize MSRV compatibility; pinned in CI A 1.70 MSRV wins when the library must be embeddable in an Android platform build or another large system that ships a specific, older stable Rust toolchain across thousands of targets
ADR-26 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (Rust standard dual-license) GPL, proprietary Matches RustCrypto ecosystem; compatible with subtle, zeroize, sha3 dependency licenses GPL wins when the implementing organization's business model requires copyleft to prevent closed-source forks from undercutting the vendor; applicable in a government-funded open-source mandate
ADR-27 Error type Crate-local enum PqcError { MalformedInput, DecodingError, VerificationFailed, KeygenFailed } anyhow, thiserror, Box<dyn Error> No heap allocation in error path; no_std compatible; thiserror for CLI layer only anyhow wins in an application-layer crate (not a library) where error context chaining and rich user-facing messages are more important than minimal heap footprint; never in a no_std library
ADR-28 Const-time integer comparison subtle::ConstantTimeEq == operator, bcmp == compiles to short-circuit branch; ConstantTimeEq emits cmov sequence; mandatory for FO ciphertext comparison and KAT byte comparison == operator with #[allow(clippy::suspicious_operation_groupings)] wins in pure-test code where the comparison is on public (non-secret) data and readability is paramount; never on secret-dependent paths

        • uniform.rsrej_uniform: XOF stream → Poly mod q via rejection
        • expand.rsexpand_a, expand_s, expand_y from seed/sigma/rho
  • │
    • pqc-encode/Phase 6 — byte serialization, compress/decompress; no_std
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rs
        • bitpack.rspoly_tobytes_d / poly_frombytes_d: d bits/coeff packing
        • compress.rscompress_q(x,d), decompress_q(x,d): Kyber rounding
        • dilithium_pack.rspack/unpack t1, t0, s1, s2, z, h per FIPS 204
  • │
    • pqc-ct/Phase 12 — constant-time primitives; no_std; no secret branch
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rs
        • cmp.rsct_eq_bytes: constant-time byte-slice equality; returns u8 mask
        • select.rsct_select: choose a or b based on mask; no branch
        • zeroize.rsSecretBox<T>, ZeroizeOnDrop; barrier + memset pattern

(…continued — pqc-kem/, pqc-sign/, pqc-kat/, pqc-cli/)

  • pqc-kem/Phases 7–8 — ML-KEM CPA PKE + FO CCA2 KEM
    • Cargo.toml
    • src/
      • lib.rs
      • cpa.rscpa_keygen, cpa_enc, cpa_dec; Module-LWE
      • kem.rskem_keygen, kem_encaps, kem_decaps; FO; implicit rejection
  • │
    • pqc-sign/Phases 9–11 — ML-DSA keygen/sign/verify
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rs
        • keygen.rssign_keygen: expand A, sample s1/s2, compute t=As1+s2, split t
        • sign.rssign: Fiat-Shamir with aborts; rejection loop; emit Signature
        • verify.rsverify: UseHint, recompute c', norm checks, hint count
        • hints.rsmake_hint, use_hint: carry-correction bit computation
  • │
    • pqc-kat/Phase 13 — KAT harness; dev/test only; depends all crates
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • lib.rs
        • rng.rsDeterministicRng: seeded ChaCha8; reproducible KAT seeds
        • runner.rsrun_kat_kem, run_kat_sign: parse JSON, call API, assert bytes
        • vectors.rsload_vectors(path) → Vec<KatVector>; JSON → KatVector
  • │
    • pqc-cli/Phase 14 — binary; subcommands; depends pqc-kem, pqc-sign, pqc-kat
      • Cargo.toml
      • src/
        • main.rsclap dispatch: keygen | encaps | decaps | sign | verify | kat
        • keygen.rs--scheme ml-kem-768|... → write pk/sk files
        • encaps.rsread pk → print ciphertext + shared secret (hex)
        • decaps.rsread sk + ciphertext → print shared secret
        • sign_cmd.rsread sk + message → print signature
        • verify_cmd.rsread pk + message + signature → VALID or INVALID
        • kat_cmd.rsrun full ACVP suite; summary report; exit 1 on any fail

11. Core Schema and Core Types

11.1 pqc-core types

// params.rs
pub struct KemParams {
    pub k: usize,       // module rank: 2 (512), 3 (768), 4 (1024)
    pub eta1: usize,    // CBD noise parameter for s, e
    pub eta2: usize,    // CBD noise parameter for e1, e2
    pub du: usize,      // compress bits for u
    pub dv: usize,      // compress bits for v
    pub q: i32,         // 3329
}

pub struct SignParams {
    pub k: usize,       // rows in A: 4 (44), 6 (65), 8 (87)
    pub l: usize,       // cols in A / length of s1
    pub eta: usize,     // bound on s1, s2 coefficients
    pub tau: usize,     // number of ±1s in challenge c
    pub beta: usize,    // ||cs1||/||cs2|| rejection bound
    pub gamma1: i32,    // masking y coefficient bound
    pub gamma2: i32,    // low-bits rounding divisor
    pub omega: usize,   // max total hint bits
    pub q: i32,         // 8380417
}

pub const ML_KEM_512:  KemParams = KemParams { k: 2, eta1: 3, eta2: 2, du: 10, dv: 4, q: 3329 };
pub const ML_KEM_768:  KemParams = KemParams { k: 3, eta1: 2, eta2: 2, du: 10, dv: 4, q: 3329 };
pub const ML_KEM_1024: KemParams = KemParams { k: 4, eta1: 2, eta2: 2, du: 11, dv: 5, q: 3329 };
pub const ML_DSA_44: SignParams = SignParams { k: 4, l: 4, eta: 2, tau: 39, beta: 78,  gamma1: 1<<17, gamma2: (q-1)/88, omega: 80, q: 8380417 };
pub const ML_DSA_65: SignParams = SignParams { k: 6, l: 5, eta: 4, tau: 49, beta: 196, gamma1: 1<<19, gamma2: (q-1)/32, omega: 55, q: 8380417 };
pub const ML_DSA_87: SignParams = SignParams { k: 8, l: 7, eta: 2, tau: 60, beta: 120, gamma1: 1<<19, gamma2: (q-1)/32, omega: 75, q: 8380417 };

(…continued — Zq, Domain, PqcError types)

// zq.rs
#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub struct Zq(pub i32);   // canonical range [0, q); stored as i32

impl Zq {
    pub fn new(v: i32, q: i32) -> Self;               // reduce v into [0, q) via barrett_reduce
    pub fn add(self, rhs: Self, q: i32) -> Self;      // (a + b) mod q, no branch
    pub fn sub(self, rhs: Self, q: i32) -> Self;      // (a - b + q) mod q, no branch
    pub fn mul(self, rhs: Self, q: i32) -> Self;      // montgomery_reduce(a * b)
    pub fn montgomery_reduce(a: i64, q: i32) -> i32;  // contract: output in (-q, q)
    pub fn barrett_reduce(a: i32, q: i32) -> i32;     // contract: output in [0, q)
}

// domain.rs
#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum Domain { Normal, Ntt }

// error.rs
#[derive(Debug)]
pub enum PqcError {
    DecapsulationFailure,  // FO mismatch (implicit rejection triggered)
    VerificationFailure,   // signature norm / hint / c~ mismatch
    EncodingError,         // byte length / bit-pack violation
    MalformedInput,        // parse failure on key / ciphertext / signature
    NormBoundExceeded,     // ||z|| or hint count out of spec
    SamplerAbortLimit,     // signing rejected too many times (> MAX_SIGN_ITER)
}
Type Ownership Mutation Concurrency Serialization Compat
KemParams Copy Immutable (const) Send+Sync N/A (compile-time) Semver-stable layout
SignParams Copy Immutable (const) Send+Sync N/A Semver-stable layout
Zq Copy None (new value) Send+Sync i32 wire (little-endian) Breaking if q changes
Domain Copy None Send+Sync 1-bit flag in metadata Stable variants
PqcError Owned None Send Debug string Non-exhaustive (#[non_exhaustive])

11.2 pqc-poly types

// poly.rs
pub struct Poly {
    pub coeffs: [i32; 256],  // coefficients in Z_q; domain tracked externally via Domain tag
}

impl Poly {
    pub fn zero() -> Self;
    pub fn add(&self, rhs: &Self, q: i32) -> Self;
    pub fn sub(&self, rhs: &Self, q: i32) -> Self;
    pub fn negate(&self, q: i32) -> Self;
    pub fn reduce_all(&mut self, q: i32);                          // I8: all coeffs → [0, q)
    pub fn schoolbook_mul(&self, rhs: &Self, q: i32) -> Self;     // reference; I5 oracle
}

// polyvec.rs
pub struct PolyVec<const K: usize> {
    pub polys: [Poly; K],
}

impl<const K: usize> PolyVec<K> {
    pub fn zero() -> Self;
    pub fn add(&self, rhs: &Self, q: i32) -> Self;
    pub fn inner_product_ntt(&self, rhs: &Self, q: i32) -> Poly;  // NTT-domain dot product
}

// polymat.rs
pub struct PolyMat<const K: usize, const L: usize> {
    pub rows: [[Poly; L]; K],
}

impl<const K: usize, const L: usize> PolyMat<K, L> {
    pub fn mul_vec_ntt(&self, v: &PolyVec<L>, q: i32) -> PolyVec<K>; // A*v in NTT domain
    pub fn mul_vec_transposed_ntt(&self, v: &PolyVec<K>, q: i32) -> PolyVec<L>; // A^T * v
}
Type Ownership Mutation Concurrency Serialization Notes
Poly Owned, stack ([i32;256]) Interior via &mut; never shared Send (not Sync if mutated) pack/unpack in pqc-encode Domain tag carried by caller
PolyVec<K> Owned, stack &mut Send K polys serialized in order K fixed at compile time
PolyMat<K,L> Owned, stack &mut Send K*L polys; public matrix A not serialized directly Expanded from Seed each time

11.3 pqc-ntt types

No new public structs; operates on Poly and PolyVec<K> in-place.

pub fn ntt_inverse(p: &mut Poly, zetas: &[i32; 128], q: i32);   // I5: left inverse of forward
// mul.rs
pub fn ntt_pointwise_mul(a: &Poly, b: &Poly, q: i32) -> Poly;   // coefficient-wise product in NTT domain
pub fn ntt_mul(a: &Poly, b: &Poly, zetas: &[i32;128], q: i32) -> Poly; // fwd(a) × fwd(b) → inv; I5
// zetas.rs
pub const ZETAS_KEM:  [i32; 128];   // primitive 256th roots for q=3329
pub const ZETAS_SIGN: [i32; 128];   // primitive 256th roots for q=8380417
Invariant Enforcement
I5: inv(fwd(p)) == p property test, every Phase 3 CI gate
I5: ntt_mul == schoolbook differential fuzz test Phase 3
No domain confusion caller must track Domain; P6
Constant-time (public data) zetas are public; no secret branching

// shake.rs
pub struct Shake128 { state: [u64; 25], rate: usize, offset: usize, finalized: bool }
pub struct Shake256 { state: [u64; 25], rate: usize, offset: usize, finalized: bool }

impl Shake128 {
    pub fn new() -> Self;
    pub fn absorb(&mut self, data: &[u8]);
    pub fn finalize(&mut self);
    pub fn squeeze(&mut self, out: &mut [u8]);  // streaming; repeatable
}
// sha3.rs
pub fn sha3_256(input: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32];
pub fn sha3_512(input: &[u8]) -> [u8; 64];
// fips_names.rs — thin wrappers with spec-aligned names
pub fn G(input: &[u8]) -> [u8; 64];             // = sha3_512
pub fn H(input: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32];             // = sha3_256
pub fn PRF(seed: &[u8; 32], nonce: u8) -> Shake256; // = SHAKE256(seed || nonce)
pub fn XOF(rho: &[u8; 32], i: u8, j: u8) -> Shake128; // = SHAKE128(rho || i || j)
Type Ownership Mutation Concurrency Persistence
Shake128/256 Owned, stack &mut self absorb/squeeze Not Send while mutating No serialization; re-derive from seed
[u8; 32/64] hash outputs Owned None after return Send+Sync Opaque bytes; caller interprets

cbd_eta1/eta2 Yes (noise) Yes (P7) PRF stream consumed word-by-word without branching on value
rej_uniform No (public A) No (P7) Loop exits on acceptance; timing varies with q
expand_a No No Seed rho is public
expand_s Yes Yes sigma is part of secret key
expand_y Yes Yes masking y must be hidden

// kem.rs
pub struct KemPublicKey  { pub encoded: Vec<u8> }                 // (t, rho) serialized per FIPS 203
pub struct KemSecretKey  { pub encoded: Vec<u8> }                 // (s || pk || H(pk) || z)
pub struct Ciphertext    { pub bytes: Vec<u8> }                   // (u, v) compressed bytes

Type Secret? Zeroize on drop? Invariant
KemSecretKey Yes Yes (P8) encodes (s ‖ pk ‖ H(pk) ‖ z); z is rejection seed
SharedSecret Yes Yes 32 bytes; I3: both sides equal on valid ct
Ciphertext No No I4: invalid ct → PRF(z, ct) returned, no error
CpaSecretKey Yes Yes s coeffs wiped after FO re-encrypt
