Phase 1 — core params + Zq modular arithmetic

1.1 Objectives

  • Establish pqc-core as the dependency-free foundation crate: parameter sets, Zq type, errors.
  • Lock constants for ML-KEM-512/768/1024 (KemParams) and ML-DSA-44/65/87 (SignParams) as const items.
  • Implement Zq arithmetic (add, sub, mul) with both Montgomery and Barrett reduction for q=3329 and q=8380417.
  • Provide constant-time canonical reduce (no branch on value); range invariant [0, q) after every operation.
  • Expose property-test harness: reduction range, Montgomery roundtrip, commutativity, distributivity.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P1 (constant-time reduce), P2 (specification-exact constants), P12 (explicit security arguments); I8 (reduction range); ADR-01 (Rust), ADR-08 (i32+i64 arithmetic); HAC (B5) sec. 14.3 (Montgomery), Longa & Naehrig 2016 (Pa-8).

File Content
pqc-core/src/params.rs KemParams + SignParams structs; all per-set constants (k, l, eta1, eta2, du, dv, q, n, omega, gamma1, gamma2, tau, beta, lambda) as const items; three KemParams instances + three SignParams instances
pqc-core/src/zq.rs Zq newtype wrapping i32; from_i32_reduce, add, sub, mul (with Montgomery), neg, canonical_reduce; two reduction engines: barrett_reduce_kyber (q=3329) and barrett_reduce_dilithium (q=8380417)
pqc-core/src/mont.rs Montgomery constants (MONT_R, MONT_R2, MONT_INV, QINV); mont_reduce(a: i64) -> i32 (the inner reduction step); pre-compute zeta_table slot reserved here (populated in Phase 3)
pqc-core/src/error.rs PqcError enum: MalformedKey, MalformedCiphertext, MalformedSignature, DecodeError, VerifyFailure, KatMismatch; no std::io dependency
pqc-core/src/lib.rs Re-exports; #![no_std] compatible; zeroize dependency gated behind feature

Reduction algorithms (described, not coded):

  • Barrett: precompute v = floor(2^k / q) for chosen k; approximate quotient via right-shift; subtract at most one multiple of q; result in [0, q).
  • Montgomery: represent a as a·R mod q; mont_reduce(t) = (t + ((t·q_inv mod R)·q)) / R; R=2^16 for q=3329, R=2^23 for q=8380417; output in (-q, q), follow with conditional add for canonical form.

Constant-time reduce: use ct_sub pattern: a - q + ((a - q) >> 31 & q) — no branch, no cmov.

Property tests: proptest on Zq::add, Zq::mul, Zq::neg; forall a,b: (a+b) in [0,q), mont_reduce(mont_reduce(a)*R^2) == a.

1.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-core compiles #[no_std]-compatible, zero external runtime dependencies (only zeroize optional).
  • cargo test -p pqc-core passes: unit tests for all six parameter sets, Barrett + Montgomery correctness, range property tests.
  • Exported KEM_512 / KEM_768 / KEM_1024 / DSA_44 / DSA_65 / DSA_87 constant references available to downstream crates.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
Zq range after canonical_reduce [0, q) for all inputs in [-2^30, 2^30]
Montgomery roundtrip from_mont(to_mont(x)) == x for 10 000 random samples per q
Barrett vs schoolbook agree for all a in [0, 2q)
Property test iterations 100 000 per operation, zero failures
cargo clippy --deny warnings clean
#[no_std] build succeeds with default-features=false

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture File Purpose
Reduction spot-checks tests/fixtures/zq_reduce.json {input, q, expected} rows for boundary values (0, 1, q-1, q, q+1, 2q-1, -1, -q)
Montgomery roundtrip table tests/fixtures/zq_mont.json 32 {a, mont_a, recovered} rows per q
Parameter set constants tests/fixtures/params.json Expected k, eta1, du, dv, ... per set; test fails if code disagrees

1.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Montgomery R mismatch KAT divergence in Phase 7/8 QINV computed for wrong R Montgomery roundtrip test fails early
Negative canonical result Downstream NTT produces out-of-range coefficients Missing + q in conditional subtraction Range property test catches a < 0 post-reduce
i32 overflow in mul Silent wrap, wrong ring element Product a*b exceeds i32 before Barrett Widen to i64 before multiply; assert-on-overflow in debug mode
q=8380417 Barrett constant Wrong reduction for ML-DSA Used Kyber constants for Dilithium path Parameter-set–tagged unit tests; two separate Barrett engines
QINV sign error mont_reduce produces wrong sign Off-by-one in modular inverse q * QINV ≡ -1 mod R assertion at startup

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • Barrett reduction: two multiplications, one shift — fits in a single ALU pipeline stage; critical for NTT inner loop in later phases.
  • Montgomery QINV computation: done at compile time (const eval); no runtime cost.
  • KemParams / SignParams structs: small (< 64 bytes each), cache-resident; pass by reference to avoid copy.
  • Avoid i128 in hot reduction paths; widen to i64 only, then narrow; i128 incurs library calls on some targets.

1.8 Edge Cases

Case Expected behavior
Input = 0 Zq(0) after reduce
Input = q Zq(0)
Input = 2q − 1 Zq(q-1)
Input = −1 Zq(q-1)
Input = i32::MIN Defined: canonical_reduce handles full i32 range
Mismatched q Zq is tagged with q at the type level (via const generic or newtype per q); mixing is a compile error

1.9 Failure Analysis

  • Reduction out of range cascades to NTT producing negative twiddle products, KEM encoding silently truncating bits, KAT divergence.
  • Detection: Phase 1 range property tests; CI fails before Phase 3.
  • Blast radius: limited to pqc-core; no I/O, no secrets at this phase.
  • Recovery: fix QINV / Barrett constant; regenerate zeta_table in Phase 3.

1.10 Operational Notes

  • pqc-core has no runtime telemetry (it is a pure math library); observability is via test output and RUST_LOG compile-feature for debug assertions.
  • In production binaries, compile with RUSTFLAGS="-C overflow-checks=yes" during development; disable for release after Phase 12 audit.
  • cargo deny check on pqc-core dependency tree; must stay minimal (no network, no crypto, no alloc unless feature-gated).

Phase 2 — polynomial ring arithmetic

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement pqc-poly: ring R_q = Z_q[X]/(X^256+1) operations over the Poly type.
  • Operations: coefficient-wise add, sub; schoolbook multiply with negacyclic reduction; coefficient reduction.
  • Introduce Domain enum (Normal, Ntt) as a type tag on Poly to prevent mixing NTT and normal-domain polynomials.
  • Schoolbook multiply is the ground-truth reference; it must agree exactly with NTT multiply (Phase 3's diff-test target).
  • No I/O; depends only on pqc-core.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P5 (schoolbook as oracle), P6 (domain typing), P12 (negacyclic convolution from Ring-LWE paper Pa-2); I5 (NTT vs schoolbook match); ADR-08 (i32+i64), ADR-10 (domain tag); Lyubashevsky, Peikert, Regev 2010 (Pa-2), FIPS 203 sec. 4.3.

File Content
pqc-poly/src/poly.rs Poly { coeffs: [i32; 256], domain: Domain } — add, sub, neg, reduce_coeffs, zero, clone; all coefficient ops use Zq via pqc-core
pqc-poly/src/schoolbook.rs schoolbook_mul(a: &Poly<Normal>, b: &Poly<Normal>) -> Poly<Normal> — O(n²) convolution with negacyclic: when index i+j >= 256, the contribution is negated (because X^256 = -1); intermediate in i64, final reduction via Barrett
pqc-poly/src/domain.rs enum Domain { Normal, Ntt } as a const-generic phantom parameter; Poly<Normal> and Poly<Ntt> are distinct types; unsafe cast between them prohibited in safe interface
pqc-poly/src/reduce.rs reduce_coeffs(p: &mut Poly<Normal>) — applies canonical_reduce to every coefficient; poly_from_i32_slice
pqc-poly/src/lib.rs Re-exports; feature alloc for PolyVec/PolyMat heap forms
pqc-poly/src/polyvec.rs PolyVec<K, D>: [Poly<D>; K]; add, sub, reduce, inner product (returns Poly<D>)

Schoolbook algorithm (description):

  • For each output coefficient r[i]: r[i] = sum_{j=0}^{255} sign(i,j) * a[j] * b[(i-j) mod 256] where sign(i,j) = -1 if (i-j) mod 256 < j (negacyclic wrap), else +1.
  • Accumulate in i64; reduce to i32 after summation; one canonical_reduce call.

Domain enforcement: the type parameter D: DomainTag is a sealed trait; mul only compiles for D=Normal; NTT-domain multiplication is in pqc-ntt.

2.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-poly compiles; cargo test -p pqc-poly passes.
  • schoolbook_mul tested against hand-computed examples; verified to implement negacyclic convolution.
  • PolyVec add/inner-product compiles; domain type errors are compile-time.

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
schoolbook_mul vs reference for degree-4 example exact coefficient match
Coefficient range after reduce_coeffs every coeff in [0, q)
Domain type confusion fails to compile (type error, not runtime panic)
schoolbook_mul(a, b) == schoolbook_mul(b, a) property test, 10 000 pairs
schoolbook_mul(1, p) == p (identity) passes for 100 random p
(a+b)*c == a*c + b*c mod negacyclic distributivity, 1 000 triples

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture File Purpose
Negacyclic spot-checks tests/fixtures/schoolbook_mul.json 10 {a_coeffs, b_coeffs, expected_coeffs} with hand-verified negacyclic wrap
Identity polynomial tests/fixtures/poly_identity.json poly_one (coeff 0 = 1, rest 0); mul test
Malformed lengths tests/fixtures/poly_bad_len.json Inputs with wrong coeff count; decode must reject

2.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Forgetting the negation on wrap schoolbook ≠ NTT multiply in Phase 3 diff-test X^256 = +1 assumed instead of −1 Diff-test fails for any non-trivial product
i32 intermediate overflow Silent wrong coefficients Accumulating 256 i32 products without widening Use i64 accumulator; enable overflow-checks in debug
Domain tag drift NTT-domain poly passed to schoolbook mul Phantom type not fully sealed Compile-time if sealed correctly; runtime if using unsafe cast
Off-by-one in negacyclic index Wrong sign on wrap-around term >= 256 vs > 256 boundary Unit test with highest-degree non-trivial products
reduce_coeffs not called after mul Downstream encoding sees out-of-range coefficients Lazy reduction pattern omitted Add debug-assert in encode (Phase 6)

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • Schoolbook is O(n²) = 65536 multiply-accumulate ops; only used as a reference, not on the critical path.
  • In testing, 1000 schoolbook muls take ~5 ms on modern hardware; acceptable for diff-test.
  • PolyVec inner product is k × schoolbook muls; k≤4 for ML-KEM-1024; still non-critical (NTT replaces it in production).
  • Stack: Poly is 256 × 4 = 1024 bytes; PolyVec<4> is 4 KB; avoid placing on stack in deep call stacks.

2.8 Edge Cases

Case Expected behavior
Zero polynomial × any Zero polynomial
All-max-coefficient polynomial Reduce without overflow (i64 accumulator)
Degree-255 monomial × degree-255 monomial Single negacyclic wrap, coeff 254 negated
PolyVec inner product with k=0 Returns zero polynomial
Construct Poly<Ntt> directly Must require explicit unsafe or constructor gated in pqc-ntt only

2.9 Failure Analysis

  • Negacyclic bug is silent in isolation; detected only in Phase 3 diff-test. If shipped undetected, every ML-KEM decryption fails (wrong message recovery) and every ML-DSA verification fails.
  • Detection: diff-test in Phase 3 is mandatory CI gate.
  • Blast radius: all of pqc-kem and pqc-sign produce wrong outputs.
  • Recovery: fix sign in schoolbook; all downstream tests re-run.

2.10 Operational Notes

  • pqc-poly has no runtime state; test via cargo test --release to catch release-mode optimization differences.
  • Enable RUSTFLAGS="-C overflow-checks=yes" in CI for debug builds to catch accumulator overflow.
  • The schoolbook implementation should carry a doc comment citing FIPS 203 sec. 4.3 / FIPS 204 sec. 2.3 (the ring definition) for auditability.

Phase 3 — NTT (the fast multiplier)

3.1 Objectives

  • Implement pqc-ntt: forward and inverse number-theoretic transform over R_q with precomputed zeta tables.
  • Cooley-Tukey butterfly for forward NTT; Gentleman-Sande butterfly for inverse NTT.
  • Pointwise multiplication in the NTT domain (element-wise product of coefficients).
  • Enforce I5: inverse_ntt(forward_ntt(p)) == p and ntt_mul(a,b) == schoolbook_mul(a,b) for all p, a, b.
  • All data-path operations constant-time (no branches on coefficient values).
  • Depends on pqc-core (Montgomery, zeta constants) and pqc-poly (Poly type, Domain tag).

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P5 (NTT is the multiplier, schoolbook is the oracle), P6 (domain tagging), P1 (no secret-dependent branches in butterfly); I5 (roundtrip identity); ADR-01, ADR-09 (static const zeta tables); Longa & Naehrig 2016 (Pa-8) — merged CT+GS butterfly, lazy Montgomery; CRYSTALS-Kyber submission §4 (Pa-3) — NTT zeta layout.

File Content
pqc-ntt/src/zeta_table.rs ZETA_KYBER: [i32; 128] — precomputed zeta^(bit_rev(i)) mod 3329 in Montgomery form; ZETA_DILITHIUM: [i32; 256] — for q=8380417; generation script gen_zetas.py documents derivation
pqc-ntt/src/ntt.rs ntt_forward(p: Poly<Normal>) -> Poly<Ntt> — in-place Cooley-Tukey; 7 layers for n=128 (Kyber) or 8 for n=256 (Dilithium); ntt_inverse(p: Poly<Ntt>) -> Poly<Normal> — Gentleman-Sande; final multiply by n^{-1} mod q (baked into last layer zeta)
pqc-ntt/src/pointwise.rs ntt_pointwise_mul(a: &Poly<Ntt>, b: &Poly<Ntt>) -> Poly<Ntt> — coefficient-wise Montgomery multiply; no reduction beyond Montgomery step
pqc-ntt/src/ntt_vec.rs ntt_vec_forward(v: PolyVec<K,Normal>) -> PolyVec<K,Ntt> and inverse; ntt_mat_vec_mul for A * s in KEM
pqc-ntt/src/lib.rs Re-exports; #[inline] on butterfly primitives

Cooley-Tukey butterfly (description):

  • Each butterfly: (a, b) -> (a + zeta*b mod q, a - zeta*b mod q).
  • Layer count: 7 (Kyber, n=256 operates as n=128 "negacyclic NTT" with degree-2 leaves); FIPS 203 Algorithm 9/10.
  • Twiddle factors drawn from ZETA_KYBER in bit-reversal order; index advances per layer.
  • After forward NTT, coefficients are products of degree-1 factors (NTT representation).

Inverse NTT / Gentleman-Sande butterfly (description):

  • Each butterfly: (a, b) -> ((a+b) mod q, zeta*(b-a) mod q).
  • Layers in reverse; ZETA_KYBER traversed in reverse.
  • Final layer folds in n^{-1} mod q so output is correctly normalized.

Diff-test harness:

  • For 10 000 random pairs (a, b): assert ntt_pointwise_mul(fwd(a), fwd(b)) inverse-NTTs to schoolbook_mul(a, b).
  • Single-polynomial roundtrip: inv_ntt(fwd(p)).coeffs == p.coeffs for 10 000 random p.

Constant-time note: no branch on coeffs[i]; all conditionals in Montgomery reduce are bitwise masks (from Phase 1 ct_sub pattern); zeta table indexed by loop variable, not secret.

3.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-ntt compiles; cargo test -p pqc-ntt passes.
  • Roundtrip test inv_ntt(fwd(p)) == p passes for random polynomials under both q values.
  • Diff-test ntt_mul == schoolbook_mul passes for 10 000 pairs.
  • ntt_mat_vec_mul available for Phase 7 consumption.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Threshold
Roundtrip inv(fwd(p)) == p 10 000 random polys, zero failures
Diff-test ntt_mul == schoolbook_mul 10 000 pairs per q, zero failures
Coefficient range after ntt_forward all in (-q, q) (Montgomery unreduced) or [0, q) (canonical) per chosen representation
Constant-time audit cargo-inspect / manual review: zero branches on coeffs[i]
Zeta table self-consistency ZETA[0] == mont(1), ZETA[1] == mont(zeta_gen^1 mod q), etc. for first 4 entries
cargo bench NTT baseline ntt_forward < 3 µs (Kyber q), < 10 µs (Dilithium q) on reference hardware

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture File Purpose
NTT roundtrip vectors tests/fixtures/ntt_roundtrip.json 8 polys: random, zero, all-one, max-coeff; input + expected restored output
NTT diff-test vectors tests/fixtures/ntt_difftest.json 20 {a, b, product_schoolbook} pairs; verify ntt_mul matches
Known NTT output tests/fixtures/ntt_known.json FIPS 203 Appendix A NTT example vector; byte-exact match required
Zeta table check tests/fixtures/zeta_table_kyber.json First 16 + last 4 entries in Montgomery form

3.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Wrong zeta generator All NTT outputs wrong; diff-test fails Primitive root of unity not 17 (Kyber) or correct root for Dilithium First entry of zeta table does not match FIPS Appendix A
Bit-reversal index error Scrambled NTT output Off-by-one in bit-reversal permutation Known-vector test diverges at first coefficient
Missing n^{-1} normalization in inv NTT Roundtrip off by factor of 256 Final layer zeta not folded in inv(fwd(p))[0] != p[0]; ratio is always 256
Layer count mismatch Kyber vs Dilithium Wrong result for one scheme n=256 negacyclic NTT has 7 layers not 8 for Kyber Add parameter-set assertion assert_eq!(NTT_LAYERS, 7) at compile time
Pointwise mul missing Montgomery de-normalization Coefficients scaled by R after multiply Forgot to fold out R in final inv-NTT Diff-test: ratio between schoolbook and ntt_mul is constant R mod q
Constant-time violation via lookup Timing side-channel on zeta table index Table indexed by secret-derived value (should never happen; zeta index is loop var) Verify loop variable is always public (layer + position)

3.7 Performance Concerns

Concern Detail Target
Cache footprint ZETA_KYBER is 512 bytes; fits in L1 Keep in .rodata; no runtime allocation
Loop unrolling 7-layer loop × 128 butterflies = 896 ops; #[inline] butterfly Let LLVM auto-vectorize; verify with -C target-cpu=native
i64 widening cost Each Montgomery step widens to i64 then narrows On x86-64 imul is fast; profile if bottleneck
ntt_mat_vec_mul K×L NTT multiplies per KEM encrypt/decrypt Cache PolyMat in NTT form at keygen; avoid recomputing per operation
Inv-NTT normalization Multiply all 256 coeffs by n_inv_mont in final step Fuse into last layer; do not add a separate pass
Branch prediction in butterfly No data-dependent branches; predictor sees perfectly linear pattern Expected IPC > 2 on out-of-order cores

3.8 Edge Cases

Case Expected behavior
Zero polynomial fwd(0) == 0 in NTT domain; inv(0) == 0
Polynomial with all coeffs = q−1 Roundtrip exact after canonical reduce
Poly<Ntt> passed to ntt_forward Compile error (Domain type mismatch)
ntt_pointwise_mul with one zero poly Returns zero poly in NTT domain
Coefficients at Montgomery boundary mont_reduce handles a=0 and a=R-1 correctly (unit tests in Phase 1)
q=8380417 NTT layer count 8 layers (not 7); gated by SignParams constant

3.9 Failure Analysis

  • Broken NTT causes every KEM and signature operation to silently produce wrong values; KAT vectors fail at Phase 13 but latent bugs detectable as early as this phase via diff-test.
  • Detection: diff-test is Phase 3 CI gate; known-vector fixture confirms FIPS conformance.
  • Blast radius: total — all of pqc-kem and pqc-sign depend on NTT correctness.
  • Recovery: fix zeta table generation script, regenerate constants, re-run diff-test; no persistent state to repair.
  • Postmortem check: compare generated ZETA_KYBER against pq-crystals/kyber reference zetas.h.

3.10 Operational Notes

  • gen_zetas.py (in pqc-ntt/tools/) must be checked in and reproducible; its output committed as zeta_table.rs generated by a build.rs step, or committed directly with a hash in comments.
  • CI: cargo test --release -p pqc-ntt (release to catch optimization-only miscompilations).
  • Benchmark: cargo bench -p pqc-ntt reports ntt_forward, ntt_inverse, ntt_mat_vec_mul_k3; track in CI to detect regression.
  • Timing instrumentation: #[cfg(feature="timing")] wrapper counts CPU cycles per NTT call using std::time::Instant; never emit per-call timings that correlate with data.

Phase 4 — SHA3 + SHAKE Symmetric Core

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement pqc-hash: Keccak-f[1600] sponge, SHA3-256/512 hash, SHAKE128/256 XOF.
  • Expose the four FIPS-named symmetric primitives: G (SHA3-512), H (SHA3-256), PRF (SHAKE256), XOF (SHAKE128).
  • Provide a streaming squeeze interface so samplers can pull bytes incrementally without allocation.
  • Validate byte-exact against NIST SHA-3 KAT and FIPS 203/204 ACVP vectors.
  • Constraint: no external Keccak dependency; own implementation to verify correctness and control constant-time properties.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P2 (byte-exact per FIPS 202/203/204), P1 (no lookup-table timing on Keccak state); I1 (KAT); ADR-06 (from-scratch Keccak); B3 Ch.12 (Serious Cryptography Keccak chapter), FIPS 202 (S3), Pa-12 (cache-timing avoidance on tables).

pqc-hash/src/keccak.rs

  • State: [u64; 25] (5×5 lane array, little-endian).
  • keccak_f1600(state: &mut [u64; 25]): 24 rounds × (θ, ρ, π, χ, ι); precompute round constants RC[24] and rotation offsets ROT[25] as const arrays.
  • Absorption: XOR message bytes into state lanes; pad per SHA-3 (0x06) or SHAKE (0x1f) domain byte, then 0x80 at rate boundary.

pqc-hash/src/sha3.rs

  • sha3_256(input: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32] — rate 136 bytes, capacity 64 bytes, domain 0x06.
  • sha3_512(input: &[u8]) -> [u8; 64] — rate 72 bytes, capacity 128 bytes, domain 0x06.
  • Single-call API; internal state zeroed after use.

pqc-hash/src/shake.rs

  • ShakeXof { state: [u64; 25], rate: usize, pos: usize, squeezing: bool } — streaming XOF.
  • ShakeXof::new(variant: ShakeVariant) -> Self — variant selects rate (SHAKE128: 168, SHAKE256: 136).
  • fn absorb(&mut self, data: &[u8]) — multi-call absorption; permutes when block full.
  • fn finalize(&mut self) — apply domain byte 0x1f + padding 0x80; transition to squeeze phase.
  • fn squeeze(&mut self, out: &mut [u8]) — pull bytes; permute when output block exhausted.
  • fn reset(&mut self) — wipe state, reset counters.

pqc-hash/src/lib.rs — public named wrappers:

G(seed: &[u8]) -> [u8; 64]          // SHA3-512; used in KEM keygen, encaps
H(data: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32]          // SHA3-256; used for H(pk), challenge hash
PRF(key: &[u8; 32], nonce: u8) -> ShakeXof   // SHAKE256 seeded with key||nonce
XOF(rho: &[u8; 32], i: u8, j: u8) -> ShakeXof  // SHAKE128 seeded with rho||i||j
  • J(z: &[u8], c: &[u8]) -> [u8; 32] — SHAKE256(z||c); FO implicit rejection PRF key derivation.

Testing (pqc-hash/tests/)

  • sha3_kat.rs: drive NIST SHA-3 short-message KAT files for SHA3-256/512.
  • shake_kat.rs: NIST SHAKE128/256 variable-output KAT.
  • fips_named.rs: cross-check G, H, PRF, XOF outputs against FIPS 203/204 ACVP intermediate vectors.

4.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-hash crate compiles; all four named functions and ShakeXof streaming API available.
  • SHA3-256/512 NIST KAT passes byte-exact.
  • SHAKE128/256 KAT passes; streaming squeeze matches single-call squeeze for same seed.
  • G, H, PRF, XOF outputs match FIPS 203/204 ACVP intermediate values.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Criterion
SHA3-256 KAT All NIST short + long message vectors pass
SHA3-512 KAT All NIST vectors pass
SHAKE128/256 KAT NIST variable-output vectors pass
G/H/PRF/XOF ACVP Byte-exact match against FIPS 203/204 ACVP intermediates
Streaming squeeze 1-byte incremental squeeze == bulk squeeze for 1000-byte output
Reset reset() then re-use produces identical output to fresh instance
State zeroization After reset(), state bytes are zero (checked via debug accessor)

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
tests/vectors/sha3_256_short.txt NIST SHA3-256 short-message KAT Hex digests
tests/vectors/sha3_512_short.txt NIST SHA3-512 short-message KAT Hex digests
tests/vectors/shake128_vot.txt SHAKE128 variable-output KAT Hex outputs
tests/vectors/shake256_vot.txt SHAKE256 variable-output KAT Hex outputs
tests/vectors/fips203_g_h.json ACVP G/H intermediate vectors JSON key-value
tests/vectors/fips204_prf_xof.json ACVP PRF/XOF intermediate vectors JSON key-value
Malformed: empty input SHA3-256("") NIST-specified 256-bit digest
Malformed: multi-block 1 MB input Reference-implementation digest

4.6 Pitfalls

  • Wrong domain byte: SHA-3 uses 0x06; SHAKE uses 0x1f. Mixing them produces silently wrong output that only KATs catch.
  • Endianness in lane packing: Keccak lanes are little-endian 64-bit; big-endian hosts require from_le_bytes. Missing this silently corrupts all output.
  • Off-by-one in rate boundary: absorbing exactly rate bytes without permuting before the next absorb; can trigger if block-aligned input is common in testing but the bug only surfaces on uncommon lengths.
  • Squeeze after non-finalized state: calling squeeze without finalize produces wrong output; the API must enforce ordering (boolean flag + debug-assert).
  • State aliasing on reset: reset() must zero-fill the full 200-byte state, not just set counters; partial zeroing leaves key material in memory (violates P8).
  • PRF nonce domain: FIPS 203 PRF takes a 33-byte input (32-byte key + 1-byte nonce); off-by-one in the concatenation breaks all subsequent sampling.

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Keccak-f[1600] is the dominant cost: 24 rounds × 5 χ-planes × 5 lanes. Inline the round function; avoid dynamic dispatch.
  • ShakeXof::squeeze called per-coefficient in samplers — minimize function-call overhead; squeeze a full block at once and buffer.
  • Rate mismatch on copy: copying partial blocks in absorb loops triggers extra branches; align internal buffer to rate.
  • No heap allocation in the critical path; all state on the stack.
  • Benchmark target: SHAKE128 throughput ≥ 400 MB/s on a modern x86-64 core (no hardware SHA3 extension assumed).

4.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty input: valid; pad immediately into rate block.
  • Input length exactly rate bytes: requires permutation before padding; tested explicitly.
  • Squeeze of 0 bytes: must not permute; must be a no-op.
  • Squeeze across multiple blocks: cumulative output matches single-call bulk squeeze.
  • Absorb called after finalize: must panic or return error; enforce in debug builds.
  • G (SHA3-512) on 32-byte seed: the primary KEM keygen call; check exact output against ACVP.

4.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Wrong domain byte KAT mismatch on first run All downstream crypto broken Fix constant; re-run KATs
Endianness bug KAT mismatch; only on big-endian hosts Host-specific build broken Add cfg-gated from_le_bytes
State not zeroed Memory forensics; not caught by functional tests Secret seed lingers in memory Add explicit zeroize in reset()
Squeeze without finalize Wrong KEM shared secret; hard to trace Silent correctness bug Add state-machine assert
SHAKE buffer overread MIRI / address sanitizer OOB read; UB Bounds-check squeeze cursor

4.10 Operational Notes

  • No runtime configuration; all parameters are compile-time constants.
  • Enable MIRI on pqc-hash in CI to catch UB in state manipulation.
  • Log (at debug level only) the number of Keccak permutations per operation to surface anomalous call counts during integration testing; never log state or output bytes.
  • The crate has zero dependencies; it is the leaf node in the workspace DAG — build it first in CI.
  • Fuzzing target: arbitrary-length absorb sequences + squeeze lengths; assert streaming == bulk and no panics.

Phase 5 — Sampling

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement pqc-sample: two samplers driving downstream key generation and encryption.
  • CBD_eta: centered binomial distribution, constant-time, used for secret/noise polynomials in ML-KEM and ML-DSA.
  • Uniform rejection sampler mod q: variable-time (public data), used for matrix A coefficients.
  • Expand A from seed rho via XOF(rho, i, j) for each (row, col) position.
  • Expand secret/noise vectors from sigma via PRF(sigma, nonce) for each polynomial.
  • Constraint: CBD paths are constant-time (secret); rejection sampler for A is explicitly variable-time (public, spec-allowed).

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P7 (public vs secret sampling), P1 (CBD constant-time), P2 (exact CBD_eta as per FIPS 203 sec. 4.2.2); I8 (coefficient range); ADR-04 (subtle CT primitives); CRYSTALS-Kyber sec. 2 (Pa-3) — CBD definition; Pa-12 (Bernstein 2005) — no lookup table on secret sampler.

pqc-sample/src/cbd.rs

  • cbd_eta1(prf_out: &[u8]) -> Poly — eta=2 (ML-KEM-512/ML-DSA-44/65): consumes 64 bytes from PRF; 256 pairs of 2-bit sums; each coeff = sum_a - sum_b in {-2,-1,0,1,2}.
  • cbd_eta2(prf_out: &[u8]) -> Poly — eta=3 (ML-KEM-1024): consumes 128 bytes.
  • Algorithm: for each coeff, extract eta bits for a and eta bits for b via bit-manipulation; compute a - b using only arithmetic (no branches on value); store in Poly::coeffs[i].
  • Constant-time: no if coeff < 0 branches; all operations are bitwise/arithmetic; validated with subtle-crate patterns.
  • sample_cbd(params: &KemParams, prf: &mut ShakeXof, nonce: u8) -> Poly — absorbs 32-byte key + nonce, squeezes required bytes, calls cbd_etaN.

pqc-sample/src/uniform.rs

  • sample_uniform_mod_q(xof: &mut ShakeXof) -> Poly — rejection sampler for ML-KEM matrix (q=3329).
  • Algorithm: squeeze 3 bytes at a time; extract two 12-bit values; accept if < q; reject otherwise; loop until 256 accepted.
  • sample_uniform_mod_q_dsa(xof: &mut ShakeXof) -> Poly — ML-DSA matrix (q=8380417); squeeze 3 bytes; extract one 23-bit value; accept if < q.
  • Variable-time: rejection branches depend only on the public XOF output, not on any secret.

pqc-sample/src/expand.rs

  • expand_a(rho: &Seed, k: usize, l: usize) -> PolyMat — k×l matrix; for row i, col j: XOF(rho, i as u8, j as u8) → sample_uniform_mod_q.
  • expand_s(sigma: &Seed, eta: u8, count: usize, offset: u8) -> PolyVec — secret/noise vector; for index n: PRF(sigma, offset + n as u8) → sample_cbd.
  • expand_mask(rho_prime: &[u8; 64], kappa: u16, l: usize) -> PolyVec — ML-DSA masking: SHAKE256(rho_prime || kappa + i) → uniform in [-gamma1+1, gamma1]; variable-time (masking vector is ephemeral, not a long-term secret post-sign; acceptable per FIPS 204).
  • All expansions return freshly allocated PolyVec/PolyMat; caller responsible for zeroizing secret outputs.

pqc-sample/src/lib.rs — re-exports; depends on pqc-hash (XOF/PRF) and pqc-poly (Poly/PolyVec/PolyMat).

Testing (pqc-sample/tests/)

  • cbd_dist.rs: 10k samples from a fixed seed; check empirical distribution matches theoretical CBD_eta mean (0) and variance (eta/4); chi-squared test.
  • uniform_range.rs: 100k samples all in [0, q).
  • expand_a_kat.rs: known-seed A matrix first row first poly matches ACVP intermediate.
  • expand_s_kat.rs: known sigma/nonce first secret poly matches ACVP intermediate.
  • ct_cbd.rs: run CBD under Miri; no secret-dependent branches.

5.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-sample crate compiles; expand_a, expand_s, sample_cbd, sample_uniform_mod_q available.
  • CBD distribution test passes; range test passes.
  • ACVP intermediate KAT for A-matrix and s-vector expansion passes.
  • MIRI clean on CBD paths.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Criterion
CBD range All coefficients in [-eta, +eta] for 1M samples
CBD distribution chi-squared p-value > 0.01 over 10k samples, mean ≈ 0, var ≈ eta/4
Uniform range All A-matrix coefficients in [0, q)
Expand A KAT ACVP first-row first-poly byte-exact match
Expand s KAT ACVP secret vector byte-exact match
MIRI No UB on CBD constant-time path
Expand_mask range All coefficients in [-gamma1, gamma1]
PRF nonce boundary nonce=255 wraps correctly; no panic

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
tests/vectors/fips203_expand_a.json ACVP A-matrix expansion KAT (all parameter sets) Poly coefficients
tests/vectors/fips203_expand_s.json ACVP secret vector expansion KAT Poly coefficients
tests/vectors/fips204_expand_a_dsa.json ML-DSA A-matrix KAT Poly coefficients
tests/vectors/fips204_expand_mask.json Mask vector expansion KAT Poly coefficients
cbd_eta1_fixed_seed.bin Fixed PRF output → known CBD poly Reference coefficients
cbd_eta2_fixed_seed.bin Fixed PRF output → known CBD_eta2 poly Reference coefficients
Malformed: xof truncated early squeeze returns < required bytes Must panic or return Err

5.6 Pitfalls

  • CBD bit extraction off by one: extracting eta bits starting from wrong bit offset produces a shifted distribution; symptoms are subtle (mean non-zero); catch with distribution test.
  • Nonce collision in s-expansion: s1 and s2 use different nonce offsets (0..L and L..L+K); if both use offset 0, keys are correlated and security collapses silently.
  • Variable-time CBD on secret data: using a lookup table indexed by coefficient value or a conditional expression is variable-time; must be pure arithmetic (add/sub/shift).
  • Rejection loop not seeded per (i,j): reusing the same XOF instance across matrix positions produces repeated coefficients; XOF must be re-initialized per position.
  • gamma1 mask overflow in Dilithium: expand_mask output must fit in i32 after centering; if gamma1=2^17, values up to 131071; coefficient arithmetic must not overflow before reduction.
  • PRF absorb length mismatch: FIPS 203 PRF input is exactly 33 bytes; absorbing 32+1 in separate calls is fine but must finalize before squeeze; omitting finalize gives wrong output.

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • expand_a dominates KEM keygen: k×l XOF instances + rejection sampling; each XOF call is a Keccak-f[1600] invocation; batch XOF squeeze to amortize.
  • CBD is called 2k+l times per keygen; each call squeezes 64–128 bytes and processes 256 coefficients; keep the hot path branch-free for consistent cache behavior.
  • PolyMat heap allocation: k×l Polys = up to 3×3×256×4 = 9 KB for ML-KEM-1024; fits in L1; avoid re-allocation.
  • Rejection rate for ML-KEM uniform sampler: ~12.5% (12-bit value; 4096 slots, q=3329 occupied); expected 1.14 iterations per coefficient; low overhead.
  • ML-DSA rejection rate: 23-bit value; q=8380417; ~(8388608-8380417)/8388608 ≈ 0.097% rejection; near-zero overhead.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • k=2, l=2 (ML-KEM-512) and k=4, l=4 (ML-KEM-1024): both matrix sizes exercised in KAT.
  • eta=2 vs eta=3: both CBD variants exercised; not parameterized by a runtime branch in the hot path.
  • nonce wrapping: nonce=255 used legitimately in large vectors; must not overflow u8 into wrong PRF input.
  • All-zero PRF output: produces all-zero CBD poly (valid, rare); must not trigger special-case handling.
  • XOF squeeze of exactly rate bytes: boundary condition exercised by generating exactly one Keccak block worth of A coefficients.

5.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Nonce offset collision s1/s2 KAT mismatch; statistical correlation in keygen output Full key compromise if deployed Fix offset; add explicit nonce-range check
CBD variable-time branch Timing measurement under valgrind/dudect Timing side-channel on secret Replace conditional with arithmetic
Wrong CBD eta Distribution test failure; variance mismatch Wrong noise level; security level degraded Parameterize eta correctly per KemParams
expand_a row/col swap A-matrix KAT mismatch Keygen/encrypt mismatch; decryption fails Fix XOF(rho, i, j) argument order
Rejection loop infinite (q bug) Hangs in test; timeout CI hang Assert acceptance rate > 0; validate q constant

5.10 Operational Notes

  • Record rejection loop iteration count (non-secret: it is a function of public A) as a histogram metric; anomalously high counts indicate a q-value bug.
  • CBD paths: instrument call count per nonce to ensure full secret-vector coverage during integration test.
  • MIRI target covers cbd.rs explicitly; add to CI cargo miri test -p pqc-sample.
  • expand_a is the only sampling path where variable-time is explicitly permitted; document this in module-level rustdoc so reviewers do not falsely flag it.
  • Secret PolyVec outputs from expand_s must be zeroized by the caller (pqc-kem, pqc-sign) after use; add #[must_use] and a drop-zeroize wrapper if the caller fails to do so.

Phase 6 — Encoding + Compression

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement pqc-encode: all serialization and deserialization of polynomial ring elements.
  • Bit-packing at d bits/coefficient for generic poly encoding (d = 1, 4, 5, 10, 11, 12).
  • Kyber Compress_q / Decompress_q: lossy rounding to du and dv bits; used in ciphertext (u, v) packing.
  • Dilithium pack/unpack for t1, t0, s1, s2, z, h; exact byte counts per FIPS 204.
  • Invariant I9: decode(encode(x)) == x for all in-range inputs; compression error within FIPS-specified bound.
  • Malformed input: short, long, or out-of-range byte buffers rejected without panic (I10).

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P2 (byte-exact FIPS 203/204 encoding), P9 (fail closed on malformed length/range); I9 (encode roundtrip), I10 (malformed input rejection); ADR-07 (custom pqc-encode, not serde); FIPS 203 sec. 5 and FIPS 204 sec. 5 (S1, S2) — normative byte layout; Pa-10 (Ducas 2018) — hint packing definition.

pqc-encode/src/bitpack.rs

  • fn encode_d(poly: &Poly, d: usize, out: &mut [u8]) — pack 256 coefficients at d bits each into 256*d/8 bytes; little-endian bit order per FIPS.
  • fn decode_d(bytes: &[u8], d: usize) -> Result<Poly, EncodeError> — unpack; check bytes.len() == 256*d/8; mask coefficients to d bits; return Err(EncodeError::BadLength) if length wrong.
  • Internal: maintain a bit cursor (byte index + bit offset); shift and OR into output; no unsafe.

pqc-encode/src/compress.rs

  • fn compress(x: i32, d: u32, q: i32) -> i32 — round(2^d * x / q) mod 2^d; uses integer arithmetic: ((x as i64 * (1i64 << d) + (q as i64 / 2)) / q as i64) as i32 & ((1 << d) - 1).
  • fn decompress(y: i32, d: u32, q: i32) -> i32 — round(q * y / 2^d): ((y as i64 * q as i64 + (1i64 << (d-1))) >> d) as i32.
  • fn compress_poly(p: &Poly, d: u32, q: i32) -> Poly — apply coefficient-wise; result in [0, 2^d).
  • fn decompress_poly(p: &Poly, d: u32, q: i32) -> Poly — apply coefficient-wise; result in [0, q).
  • fn encode_u(u: &PolyVec, du: u32, out: &mut Vec<u8>) — compress + pack each poly at du bits.
  • fn decode_u(bytes: &[u8], k: usize, du: u32) -> Result<PolyVec, EncodeError>.
  • fn encode_v(v: &Poly, dv: u32, out: &mut Vec<u8>) — compress + pack v at dv bits.
  • fn decode_v(bytes: &[u8], dv: u32) -> Result<Poly, EncodeError>.

pqc-encode/src/kem_keys.rs

  • fn encode_pk(rho, t) -> Vec<u8> / fn decode_pk(bytes, k) -> Result<(Seed, PolyVec), EncodeError> — public key: t at 12 bits/coeff + rho (32 bytes); total 384k+32 bytes.
  • fn encode_sk(sk) -> Vec<u8> / fn decode_sk(bytes, k) -> Result<KemSecretKey, EncodeError> — s at 12 bits + pk bytes + H(pk) + z.

pqc-encode/src/dsa_keys.rs

  • t1 at 10 bits/coeff (FIPS 204 sec. 5.4); t0 at 13 bits (signed, centered); s1/s2 at ceil(log2(2*eta+1)) bits; z at 18 bits (gamma1=2^17) or 20 bits (gamma1=2^19); h packed as (number_of_ones: u8, position_list: [u8; omega]).
  • fn pack_signature(sig, params) -> Vec<u8> / fn unpack_signature(bytes, params) -> Result<Signature, EncodeError>.

pqc-encode/src/error.rs — enum EncodeError { BadLength, OutOfRange, MalformedHint }.

Testing (pqc-encode/tests/)

  • roundtrip.rs: for d in {1,4,5,10,11,12}, random poly → encode → decode == original (proptest); compress_error.rs: max |decompress(compress(x, d, q), d, q) - x| ≤ q/2^(d+1) for 10k random x.
  • kem_key_roundtrip.rs / dsa_key_roundtrip.rs: random key/signature → pack → unpack == original, for both KEM and DSA types.
  • malformed.rs: truncated / extended / empty byte slices return Err not panic.
  • acvp_encode.rs: FIPS 203/204 ACVP public-key and ciphertext byte sequences match encoded outputs.

6.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-encode crate compiles; all encode/decode and compress/decompress functions available.
  • Roundtrip property test passes for all bit widths.
  • Compression error within FIPS bound.
  • ML-KEM public key and ciphertext byte layout matches ACVP.
  • ML-DSA signature byte layout matches ACVP.
  • Malformed inputs return EncodeError, no panics.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Criterion
Bitpack roundtrip proptest 10k cases; decode(encode(p, d), d) == p for d ∈ {1,4,5,10,11,12}
Compress error max error ≤ floor(q / 2^(d+1)) for all (x, d) pairs
KEM PK byte length 384k+32 bytes (k=2: 800, k=3: 1184, k=4: 1568)
KEM SK byte length FIPS 203 Table 2 exact sizes per parameter set
Ciphertext byte length FIPS 203 Table 2 exact (kdu32 + dv*32 bytes)
DSA signature length FIPS 204 Table 2 exact per parameter set
Malformed length Err(EncodeError::BadLength) returned, no panic
Malformed hint Err(EncodeError::MalformedHint) when hint positions unsorted or count > omega
ACVP byte-exact PK, SK, ciphertext, signature match FIPS 203/204 ACVP encoding test vectors

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
tests/vectors/fips203_pk_enc.json ML-KEM PK encoding KAT (all parameter sets) Hex byte strings
tests/vectors/fips203_ct_enc.json Ciphertext (u, v) encoding KAT Hex byte strings
tests/vectors/fips204_pk_enc.json ML-DSA PK encoding KAT Hex byte strings
tests/vectors/fips204_sig_enc.json Signature encoding KAT Hex byte strings
malformed/{truncated_kem_pk,oversized_sig}.bin 1 byte short ML-KEM-512 PK; 1 byte over ML-DSA-44 signature Both must return BadLength
malformed/{bad_hint_order,out_of_range_t1}.bin Hint positions not strictly increasing; t1 coefficient ≥ 2^10 MalformedHint; OutOfRange respectively

6.6 Pitfalls

  • Compress rounding bias: naive x * 2^d / q truncates; must use round-half-up with + q/2 before division; off-by-one causes decryption failure in ML-KEM.
  • Bit cursor alignment in bitpack: packing non-power-of-2 widths (e.g., 10 bits) across byte boundaries is error-prone; unit-test each bit-width boundary case individually.
  • t0 signed packing: t0 coefficients are signed and centered; encode as unsigned offset (+ 2^(d-1)) before packing; omitting the shift produces wrong unpacked values.
  • Hint position invariant: FIPS 204 requires hint positions within each polynomial to be strictly increasing; accepting unsorted positions in unpack opens a malleability vector.
  • PolyVec length mismatch in decode_u: if k is wrong (e.g., caller passes k=3 for a k=2 ciphertext), decode silently pads; must validate bytes.len() == k * 32 * du.
  • Integer overflow in compress: x * (1 << d) with x up to q-1=3328 and d=12: 3328 * 4096 = 13,631,488 fits in i32; for q=8380417 and d=10: 8380417 * 1024 = 8,581,547,008 — overflows i32 (max 2,147,483,647); must use i64 intermediate.

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • Bitpack inner loop: 256 coefficients × d bits; unrolling at compile time (const generic on d) avoids runtime branches.
  • Compress_poly: 256 multiplications + divisions; division is expensive; replace with a multiply-and-shift approximation only if KAT still passes (risky; keep division unless profiling shows it is a bottleneck).
  • encode_pk / decode_pk called once per keygen/decaps; not on the critical-latency path; straightforward allocation acceptable.
  • Signature packing called once per sign; z packing (256 × 18–20 bits) and hint packing are the inner loops (both under 1 µs at target); avoid Vec<u8> re-allocation during encode by pre-sizing with Vec::with_capacity(known_size).

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Zero polynomial: all-zero encoding must round-trip.
  • Coefficient at q-1 (maximum): compress then decompress; error within bound.
  • Coefficient at boundary of compress domain: x = q/2; rounding choice must match FIPS 203 spec exactly.
  • Hint boundary conditions: 0 ones (valid; no positions, just omega-byte zero list), exactly omega ones (maximum valid hint; positions at [0..omega-1]), omega+1 ones (must return MalformedHint).
  • Partial-block bit-packing: 256 coefficients at 10 bits = 2560 bits = 320 bytes exactly; no partial final byte — edge case disappears but worth asserting.

6.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Rounding error in compress Decryption failure rate spikes; KAT mismatch ML-KEM decaps fails Fix rounding formula; re-run KAT
Signed t0 shift omitted DSA key roundtrip fails; ACVP mismatch Signature verify fails Add centering offset in pack/unpack
Hint malleability accepted Signature with non-canonical hint accepted Signature forgery vector Strict monotone check in unpack
i32 overflow in compress Wrong compressed value; subtle; only large-q hit ML-DSA encoding wrong Switch intermediate to i64
decode_u k mismatch Decaps with wrong k; shared secret wrong Interop failure Validate length pre-decode

6.10 Operational Notes

  • Encoding/decoding is deterministic and stateless; no runtime configuration.
  • Instrument total bytes serialized per operation (KEM vs DSA) as a counter metric for throughput monitoring.
  • The EncodeError enum must be part of the public API surface documented in rustdoc; downstream crates (pqc-kem, pqc-sign) propagate it.
  • Fuzz target: random byte slices of plausible lengths into all decode_* functions; assert no panic, only Ok or Err.
  • The exact byte-length constants for each parameter set should be encoded as const values in pqc-core and cross-checked in decode_* entry points; mismatch is a compile-time-detectable error.

Phase 7 — ML-KEM CPA PKE

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement the CPA-secure Module-LWE public-key encryption layer in pqc-kem.
  • Cover all three parameter sets: ML-KEM-512 (k=2), ML-KEM-768 (k=3), ML-KEM-1024 (k=4).
  • All matrix-vector products computed via NTT (pqc-ntt); schoolbook forbidden in production paths.
  • Output byte representations must match FIPS 203 sec. 6 encodings exactly.
  • Constraint: no randomness source injected from outside; keygen/enc accept explicit seeds for KAT reproducibility.

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P2 (spec-exact), P5 (NTT only in production paths), P6 (Domain tag prevents normal/NTT confusion); I3 (KEM correctness), I5 (NTT identity), I8 (coefficient range); ADR-01, ADR-08, ADR-11; CRYSTALS-Kyber sec. 2 (Pa-3) — CPA PKE algorithms; FIPS 203 sec. 6 (S1) — normative algorithm.

Module/file: pqc-kem/src/cpa.rs

Keygen (cpa_keygen):

  1. Derive (rho, sigma) = G(d) where d is 32-byte random seed; G = SHA3-512 (pqc-hash).
  2. Expand A: PolyMat (k×k) from rho via expand_a (pqc-sample XOF-per-(i,j)).
  3. Sample s: PolyVec (k polys) from sigma using CBD_eta1; sample e: PolyVec from sigma with offset.
  4. NTT-forward all of s, e, each row of A.
  5. Compute t_hat = A_hat * s_hat + e_hat (all in NTT domain; pointwise mul + poly add per row).
  6. Inverse-NTT t_hat → t; encode t and rho → KemPublicKey bytes (pqc-encode).
  7. Secret key bytes = encoded s + public-key bytes (needed by CCA2 layer).

Encrypt (cpa_enc):

  1. Expand A^T from rho (transpose indexing in expand_a).
  2. Sample r: PolyVec from randomness seed via CBD_eta1; sample e1: PolyVec (CBD_eta2); sample e2: Poly (CBD_eta2).
  3. NTT-forward r, A^T, compute u_hat = A_hat^T * r_hat + e1_hat.
  4. Compute v = t^T * r_hat (inv-NTT result) + e2 + Decompress_q(m, 1).
  5. Compress u to du bits/coeff, v to dv bits → Ciphertext bytes (pqc-encode).

Decrypt (cpa_dec):

  1. Decode u (decompress from du), v (from dv); decode s from secret key.
  2. NTT-forward u, s; compute s_hat^T * u_hat; inv-NTT → su.
  3. m = Compress_q(v - su, 1) → 32 bytes.

Signatures across layers:

fn cpa_keygen(params: &KemParams, d: &Seed) -> (KemPublicKey, CpaSecretKey);
fn cpa_enc(params: &KemParams, pk: &KemPublicKey, m: &[u8;32], r: &Seed) -> Ciphertext;
fn cpa_dec(params: &KemParams, sk: &CpaSecretKey, ct: &Ciphertext) -> [u8;32];

Internal helpers: ntt_matvec_mul, ntt_vec_inner, both delegating to pqc-ntt; compress_poly_vec, decompress_poly_vec in pqc-encode.

Test harness: cpa_kat.rs — deterministic seeds, compare keygen/enc/dec byte output against hand-computed FIPS 203 Appendix B vectors.

7.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-kem compiles with CPA PKE for all three parameter sets.
  • pqc-cli keygen --kem ml-kem-512 emits public and secret key bytes.
  • Decrypt(Encrypt(m)) == m for 10,000 randomly sampled messages per parameter set.
  • cargo test -p pqc-kem cpa passes; CPA KAT vectors match.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
CPA correctness Decrypt(Encrypt(m)) == m, all k, 10k trials
KAT byte-exact Matches FIPS 203 Appendix B for 512/768/1024
Decryption failure < 2^-139 per FIPS 203 analysis
NTT usage No schoolbook path active in keygen/enc/dec
I8 (reduction range) All coefficients in [0, q) after each step
I9 (roundtrip) Compress→Decompress error within FIPS bound
No unsafe cargo deny / forbid(unsafe_code) in cpa.rs

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Content Assertion
kat/kem512_cpa.json FIPS 203 Appendix B vectors for ML-KEM-512 pk, sk, ct, m byte-exact
kat/kem768_cpa.json ML-KEM-768 vectors same
kat/kem1024_cpa.json ML-KEM-1024 vectors same
kat/cpa_malformed_ct.bin truncated/padded ciphertext dec returns without panic
kat/cpa_wrong_sk.bin mismatch pk/sk pair dec output differs from m

7.6 Pitfalls

  • Transpose of A forgotten in encrypt. A was expanded in keygen as A[i][j]; encrypt must use A^T[i][j] = A[j][i]. Symptom: decryption failure every time. Debug: print intermediate t^T*r and compare against FIPS trace.
  • NTT domain mismatch. Multiplying a Normal-domain poly with an NTT-domain poly silently produces wrong output. I5 / Domain enum must gate this. Debug: enable Domain assertion; differential test against schoolbook.
  • Wrong eta per step. ML-KEM-512 uses eta1=3 for s/e, eta2=2 for e1/e2; 768/1024 use eta1=2. Using wrong eta shifts CBD width. Symptom: KAT mismatch. Debug: log sampled coefficient histograms.
  • Off-by-one in PRF nonce. CBD sampling uses PRF(sigma || N) with N incremented per polynomial. Reusing N or starting at wrong index produces wrong noise. Debug: compare per-polynomial byte streams against reference.
  • Compression rounding direction. Compress_q uses round(x * 2^d / q) mod 2^d; floor instead of round shifts KAT outputs. Debug: unit-test single coefficient compress/decompress.
  • Public key encoding byte order. Polynomial coefficients packed LSB-first at 12 bits per FIPS 203 sec. 4.2.1; any deviation causes KAT failure. Debug: decode known pk and compare individual Zq values.

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • NTT forward on A expansion: A is k×k polys; forward-NTT per row is hot path; cache twiddle factors in a static array (pqc-ntt).
  • Matrix-vector multiply: k^2 NTT pointwise muls per keygen/enc; optimize loop ordering for cache (row-major PolyMat).
  • Stack usage: PolyMat for k=4 is 4×4×256×4 = 16 KB; avoid stack allocation of full matrix; use heap-boxed or iterator construction.
  • Compression: Inner loops over 256 coefficients; keep in registers; avoid per-coeff branching.
  • Seed expansion (expand_a): SHAKE128 squeezed per (i,j) block; reuse XOF state object across coefficients within one block (do not re-init per coefficient).

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Ciphertext length not matching du*k*256/8 + dv*256/8: reject with Error::MalformedCiphertext (I10).
  • Public key shorter than 12*k*256/8 + 32: reject with Error::MalformedKey.
  • All-zero message: valid; no special path.
  • All-zero randomness seed r: valid for testing; must still produce correct output.
  • Parameter k mismatch between keygen sk and enc ciphertext: detect via encoded length mismatch; return error.

7.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Wrong NTT twiddle table I5 diff-test schoolbook vs NTT All CPA operations silently wrong Regenerate twiddle; I5 test gates CI
Compress constant wrong KAT mismatch All KAT failures for affected param Fix constant; re-run kat fixture
Stack overflow (large k) Crash on keygen CLI unusable Box PolyMat; set RUST_MIN_STACK if needed
Panic on short ciphertext Slice index OOB CLI abort Length-check before decode (I10)

7.10 Operational Notes

  • Instrument cpa_keygen, cpa_enc, cpa_dec with operation counters (pqc-ct, aggregate only).
  • Log parameter set in debug build; suppress in release.
  • pqc-cli exits non-zero on Error::MalformedKey / Error::MalformedCiphertext; error message names the parameter set.
  • No secret values in logs; log only byte lengths and parameter identifiers.
  • KAT fixture paths configurable via PQC_KAT_DIR env var for CI override.

Phase 8 — ML-KEM CCA2 KEM (Fujisaki-Okamoto)

8.1 Objectives

  • Lift the Phase 7 CPA PKE to a CCA2-secure KEM using the Hofheinz-Hovelmanns-Kiltz (HHK) / FIPS 203 Fujisaki-Okamoto transform.
  • Produce KemPublicKey, KemSecretKey, Ciphertext, SharedSecret types for all three parameter sets.
  • Decapsulation is constant-time: ciphertext comparison and key selection must not branch on comparison result (I2, P3).
  • Implicit rejection (I4): invalid ciphertext yields PRF(z, c), never an error return that reveals validity.
  • KAT output must match FIPS 203 ACVP vectors byte-for-byte (I1).

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P1 (CT on FO comparison), P3 (CCA2 via FO), P8 (zeroize m', K', r'); I2 (timing independence), I4 (implicit rejection); ADR-04 (subtle CT), ADR-05 (zeroize); Fujisaki & Okamoto 1999 (Pa-5) — FO transform; Hofheinz, Hövelmanns, Kiltz 2017 (Pa-6) — QROM proof of implicit rejection; Avanzi et al. 2022 IEEE S&P (Pa-9) — exact G(m,H(pk)) construction.

Module/file: pqc-kem/src/kem.rs

KEM Keygen (kem_keygen):

  1. Call cpa_keygen(params, d) → (ek_pke, dk_pke).
  2. Compute H_pk = H(ek_pke) (SHA3-256 of encoded public key bytes).
  3. Sample rejection seed z (32 random bytes).
  4. KemSecretKey = dk_pke || ek_pke || H_pk || z (concatenation per FIPS 203 sec. 7.3).
  5. KemPublicKey = ek_pke.

Encapsulation (kem_encaps):

  1. Sample random message m (32 bytes) from CSPRNG.
  2. (K, r) = G(m || H_pk) (SHA3-512 split; G = pqc-hash G function).
  3. c = cpa_enc(params, pk, m, r).
  4. Output (c, K) as (Ciphertext, SharedSecret).

Decapsulation (kem_decaps) — constant-time critical path:

  1. Parse sk: extract dk_pke, ek_pke, H_pk, z.
  2. m' = cpa_dec(params, dk_pke, c).
  3. (K', r') = G(m' || H_pk).
  4. c' = cpa_enc(params, pk, m', r') (re-encryption).
  5. eq: u8 = ct_eq_bytes(c, c') (pqc-ct; constant-time; result is 0xFF or 0x00).
  6. K_bad = PRF(z, c) (SHAKE256 keyed on z with c as input, 32-byte output).
  7. K_out = ct_select(eq, K', K_bad) (pqc-ct conditional move; no branch).
  8. Zeroize m', K', K_bad, r' on exit (P8).
  9. Return K_out.

Key signatures:

fn kem_keygen(params: &KemParams, rng: &mut dyn CryptoRng) -> (KemPublicKey, KemSecretKey);
fn kem_encaps(params: &KemParams, pk: &KemPublicKey, rng: &mut dyn CryptoRng)
    -> Result<(Ciphertext, SharedSecret), Error>;
fn kem_decaps(params: &KemParams, sk: &KemSecretKey, ct: &Ciphertext)
    -> SharedSecret;  // never errors; implicit rejection

pqc-ct additions: ct_eq_bytes(a: &[u8], b: &[u8]) -> u8, ct_select(flag: u8, t: &[u8;32], f: &[u8;32]) -> [u8;32].

KAT harness (pqc-kat/src/kem_kat.rs): inject deterministic RNG (counter-mode); drive encaps+decaps; assert shared-secret bytes match ACVP vectors.

8.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-cli encaps --kem ml-kem-768 --pk pk.bin outputs ciphertext + shared secret.
  • pqc-cli decaps --kem ml-kem-768 --sk sk.bin --ct ct.bin outputs shared secret.
  • kem_decaps returns a 32-byte value for any byte input (including random garbage) — never panics, never errors.
  • All FIPS 203 ACVP KAT vectors pass for 512/768/1024.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
I1 KAT exact ACVP shared-secret bytes match for all 3 param sets
I3 KEM correctness decaps(sk, encaps(pk)) == encaps-output SharedSecret
I4 Implicit rejection tampered ciphertext → SharedSecret differs, no error
I2 Constant-time no branch on eq; validated with timing harness
Decaps panic-free 10k random-garbage ciphertexts → no panic
Zeroization miri / valgrind find no post-drop secret reads
Secret key length == `

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Content Assertion
kat/kem512_kat.json FIPS 203 ACVP ML-KEM-512 encaps/decaps vectors K byte-exact
kat/kem768_kat.json ML-KEM-768 K byte-exact
kat/kem1024_kat.json ML-KEM-1024 K byte-exact
kat/kem_tampered_ct.bin flip one byte of valid ciphertext decaps returns K_bad ≠ K_good
kat/kem_random_ct.bin 32 random-garbage ciphertexts no panic, returns 32 bytes each
kat/kem_short_sk.bin truncated secret key Error::MalformedKey on parse

8.6 Pitfalls

  • Branching on ct_eq result. Any if eq { return K' } else { return K_bad } pattern is a timing oracle breaking CCA2. Symptom: timing side-channel; unit test passes. Fix: use ct_select; audit with constant-time-analyzer.
  • Re-encryption uses same pk bytes. cpa_enc must use ek_pke stored in sk, not a caller-supplied pk. Mixing allows mismatch attacks. Debug: assert pointer equality or byte-compare in test.
  • G split point off by one. G(m||H_pk) = SHA3-512; output[0..32] = K, output[32..64] = r. Swapping K/r produces wrong shared secret silently. Debug: print intermediate G output in KAT mode.
  • PRF(z, c) wrong construction. FIPS 203 defines J(z, c) = SHAKE256(z || c, 32); some implementations use HMAC or reversed order. Debug: KAT for tampered-ct case.
  • z not included in secret key serialization. z is appended last; off-by-one in sk layout causes deserialization of wrong z. Debug: parse sk and compare z field against known vector.
  • Zeroization eliminated by compiler. Rust ptr::write_volatile or the zeroize crate required; plain = 0 is optimized out. Verify with Miri.
  • encaps returning error leaks validity. kem_encaps should only fail on malformed pk (length); it must not return Err when randomness is fine. Caller never learns if pk is "good enough" to encapsulate.

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • Re-encryption in decaps: full cpa_enc call on every decapsulation — same cost as encaps; unavoidable; budget ~1 ms for k=3 on modern CPU.
  • SHA3-512 for G: single call per encaps/decaps; not a bottleneck; ensure pqc-hash uses optimized Keccak (no per-byte API).
  • ct_eq_bytes: compare |c| bytes (≤1568 bytes for k=4) constant-time; use SIMD-friendly word-at-a-time XOR-fold; verify no early exit with compiler explorer.
  • Zeroize cost: zeroize on drop of secret buffers is negligible; batch into one call per scope.
  • Stack depth: decaps calls cpa_dec + cpa_enc in sequence; both allocate PolyMat; keep boxed to avoid large stack frames.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Ciphertext length != expected per params: parse error; decaps returns PRF(z, random_ct_padded) or equivalent per spec's handling.
  • Secret key length mismatch: return Error::MalformedKey before any crypto.
  • Public key all-zeros is valid input (keygen rejects only a degenerate seed, not an all-zero pk); encaps with a public key from a different parameter set is caught by length mismatch at parse.
  • Concurrent encaps from multiple threads is safe (stateless, no shared mutable state); m = all-zeros is valid (G produces deterministic but valid (K, r)).

8.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Timing branch in decaps Timing harness (Phase 12) CCA2 broken: key recovery possible Replace if-branch with ct_select; re-audit
Wrong G split KAT mismatch All KEM operations produce wrong K Fix byte range; all KAT suites rerun
Zeroize no-op Miri secret-after-drop Secret leaks in long-lived processes Use zeroize crate; Miri in CI
Panic on short ct Slice OOB in cpa_dec CLI crash on adversarial input Length-gate before decode
z lost in sk serialization KAT tampered-ct case fails Implicit rejection broken Fix sk layout parser/writer; fixture regression

8.10 Operational Notes

  • kem_decaps MUST NOT log whether ct matched; log only: {operation: "decaps", param_set: "ML-KEM-768", ok: true}.
  • Counters: pqc_kem_encaps_total, pqc_kem_decaps_total — increment regardless of implicit-rejection branch.
  • CLI: --output-format hex|bin; shared secret is 32 bytes; default output is hex.
  • Secret key files: recommend 0600 permissions; document in CLI help.
  • Rollback: if a parameter set is found defective, KemParams is a runtime value; swap constant without ABI change.

Phase 9 — ML-DSA Key Generation

9.1 Objectives

  • Implement sign_keygen in pqc-sign for all three ML-DSA parameter sets: 44 (l=4,k=4), 65 (l=5,k=6), 87 (l=7,k=8).
  • Produce SignPublicKey = (rho, t1) and SignSecretKey = (rho, K, tr, s1, s2, t0).
  • Power2Round splits t into t1 (high bits, ≥d-1 bits) and t0 (low bits, d=13 bits per FIPS 204).
  • All matrix-vector products via NTT; secrets sampled from bounded distribution (eta=2 or 4 per param set).
  • Output bytes must match FIPS 204 ACVP known-answer vectors (I1).

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P2 (byte-exact t1/t0 split per FIPS 204), P8 (zeroize s1, s2, t0); I1 (KAT exact), I6 (sign correctness precondition); ADR-01, ADR-11; CRYSTALS-Dilithium sec. 2 (Pa-4) — keygen algorithm; FIPS 204 sec. 6.1 (S2) — Power2Round definition; Pa-10 (Ducas 2018) — HighBits/LowBits derivation.

Module/file: pqc-sign/src/keygen.rs

sign_keygen(params: &SignParams, xi: &Seed) -> (SignPublicKey, SignSecretKey):

  1. (rho, rho_prime, K) = H(xi, 128) — SHAKE256 of xi, output 96 bytes; split 32/64/32.
  2. A_hat: PolyMat (k×l) expanded from rho via expand_a (pqc-sample, XOF SHAKE128 per (i,j)).
  3. s1: PolyVec (l polys) sampled from rho_prime via expand_s(rho_prime, 0..l, eta) (CBD or uniform-mod-(2*eta+1) per FIPS 204 sec. 8.3).
  4. s2: PolyVec (k polys) sampled via expand_s(rho_prime, l..l+k, eta).
  5. NTT-forward s1_hat; compute t_hat = A_hat * s1_hat; inv-NTT → t; add s2 → t = t + s2.
  6. (t1, t0) = Power2Round(t, d) — per-coefficient: t1[i] = (t[i] + 2^(d-1)) >> d, t0[i] = t[i] - t1[i]*2^d.
  7. Encode (rho, t1) → SignPublicKey bytes (pqc-encode pack_pk).
  8. tr = H(pk_bytes, 64) — SHAKE256 of encoded public key, 64 bytes.
  9. Encode (rho, K, tr, s1, s2, t0) → SignSecretKey bytes (pqc-encode pack_sk).

Key signatures:

fn sign_keygen(params: &SignParams, xi: &Seed) -> (SignPublicKey, SignSecretKey);
fn power2round(t: &PolyVec, d: u32) -> (PolyVec, PolyVec);  // returns (t1, t0)
fn expand_s(rho_prime: &[u8;64], index: usize, eta: u32) -> Poly;
fn pack_pk(rho: &Seed, t1: &PolyVec) -> Vec<u8>;
fn pack_sk(rho: &Seed, K: &[u8;32], tr: &[u8;64], s1: &PolyVec, s2: &PolyVec, t0: &PolyVec) -> Vec<u8>;

pqc-encode additions:

  • pack_t1: coefficients in [0, 2^(q_bits - d)) packed at (q_bits - d) bits each (10 bits for ML-DSA).
  • pack_t0: signed coefficients in (-2^(d-1), 2^(d-1)] packed at d bits (13 bits).
  • pack_s: coefficients in [-eta, eta] packed at ceil(log2(2*eta+1)) bits.

Verification of correctness:

  • Reconstruct t = t1*2^d + t0; verify equals original t.
  • NTT(t1) * 2^d == NTT(t) - NTT(t0) (used in Phase 11 verify).

Test harness: keygen_kat.rs in pqc-kat; deterministic xi; compare pk, sk bytes against FIPS 204 Appendix A.

9.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-cli keygen --dsa ml-dsa-65 emits pk.bin and sk.bin.
  • sign_keygen compiles for all three parameter sets with parameterized l, k, eta, d.
  • power2round round-trips: t1*2^d + t0 == t for 100k random polynomials.
  • FIPS 204 Appendix A KAT vectors match for 44/65/87.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
I1 KAT exact pk and sk bytes match FIPS 204 ACVP for all 3 param sets
t1+t0 roundtrip t1*2^d + t0 == t, all coefficients, 100k trials
Norm bounds `
`

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Content Assertion
kat/dsa44_keygen.json FIPS 204 ACVP ML-DSA-44 keygen vectors pk, sk byte-exact
kat/dsa65_keygen.json ML-DSA-65 pk, sk byte-exact
kat/dsa87_keygen.json ML-DSA-87 pk, sk byte-exact
kat/dsa_power2round.bin 1000 (t, d) pairs with expected (t1,t0) round-trip + split correctness
kat/dsa_short_xi.bin 16-byte seed (too short) Error::MalformedKey

9.6 Pitfalls

  • Wrong d bits for ML-DSA. d=13 for all ML-DSA parameter sets (unlike some draft versions using d=14). KAT immediately detects this via pk bytes.
  • rho_prime width. FIPS 204 sec. 6.1: rho_prime is 64 bytes (not 32); using 32 shifts all subsequent expand_s indices. Debug: compare rho_prime hex against known vector.
  • expand_s uses SHAKE256, not SHAKE128. Matrix A uses SHAKE128 (XOF); secrets use SHAKE256 (PRF). Mixing produces wrong s1/s2. Debug: confirm hash function per FIPS 204 Table 1.
  • Power2Round sign convention. t0 = t - t1*2^d may be negative; must be stored as signed integer in range (-2^(d-1), 2^(d-1)]; treating as unsigned shifts pack_t0 encoding. Debug: unit-test with t=0, t=1, t=2^d-1, t=2^d.
  • tr length. tr = H(pk, 64) is 64 bytes (ML-DSA) vs H(pk) = 32 bytes (ML-KEM); confusion causes off-by-32 in sk layout. Debug: print sk field offsets.
  • K field placement. sk = rho || K || tr || ...; K is 32 bytes at offset 32, before tr at offset 64. Misplacing K breaks sign and verify. Debug: parse sk per-field and compare against known vector.
  • eta=4 packing width. ML-DSA-44 uses eta=4 (3 bits/coeff); ML-DSA-65/87 use eta=2 (3 bits/coeff also, but range differs). A wrong packing width silently truncates coefficients. Debug: decode packed s1 and check range.

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • k×l matrix expansion: k*l SHAKE128 streams per keygen; ML-DSA-87 has k=8, l=7 → 56 streams. Squeeze per-poly block (34 bytes per rejection-sampled coefficient batch) minimizes Keccak permutations.
  • A_hat * s1_hat: k×l NTT pointwise muls; same loop structure as pqc-kem but larger (up to 8×7). Row-major PolyMat access.
  • Power2Round: trivial; 256*k coefficient operations; no bottleneck.
  • pack_sk: 13-bit packing of t0 over k*256 coefficients; use bitwise shift-and-mask; avoid per-coeff bounds checks in release.
  • Stack: PolyMat[8][7] = 87256*4 = 57 KB; must be heap-boxed.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • xi seed all-zeros: valid; produces deterministic keys; used in KAT.
  • Parameter set mismatch between pk and sk: detect via byte-length check on parse; return Error::MalformedKey.
  • t0 coefficient exactly at boundary 2^(d-1): inclusive on one side per FIPS 204; verify sign convention matches.
  • Decoding a pk with wrong t1 bit-width: length mismatch detected; return error (I10).
  • s1/s2 coefficients at +-eta: valid; must not be rejected by any range check.

9.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast Radius Recovery
Wrong rho_prime length KAT sk mismatch All sign/verify fail Fix SHAKE256 output length; re-run KAT
d=14 vs d=13 pk bytes wrong KAT failure; t1/t0 split incorrect Fix constant in SignParams; property test
PolyMat stack overflow Crash in keygen CLI unusable Box PolyMat allocation
expand_s wrong hash KAT s1/s2 mismatch Sign/verify broken Audit hash function per FIPS 204 Table 1
tr off by field KAT sk mismatch at offset 64 Sign fails (tr used in challenge) Fix sk serialization offsets; field-parse fixture

9.10 Operational Notes

  • Log only: {operation: "dsa_keygen", param_set: "ML-DSA-65", pk_len: N, sk_len: M}.
  • No field values of sk in logs; zeroize s1, s2, t0 if discarded before use in sign.
  • CLI: output pk and sk to separate files or combined binary (flag --split).
  • pk is public and may be freely distributed; sk must be 0600.
  • SignParams is a static, compile-time-selectable struct; swapping parameter set requires recompile or runtime dispatch; document both paths.
  • Instrument sign_keygen with a counter; no timing metrics that correlate with secret coefficient values.

Phase 10 — ML-DSA Signing (Fiat-Shamir with Aborts)

10.1 Objectives

  • Implement pqc_sign::sign for all three ML-DSA parameter sets (44, 65, 87).
  • Execute the full Fiat-Shamir-with-aborts loop: mask sampling → commitment → challenge → response → rejection test → hint computation.
  • Produce Signature { c_tilde, z, h } matching FIPS 204 byte layout exactly (I1, I7).
  • All operations on y, z, s1, s2, t0 must be constant-time per P1, I2; rejection sampling must preserve zero-knowledge so no secret leaks through loop count or timing.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P4 (rejection sampling preserves ZK), P1 (CT on z bounds check), P8 (zeroize y, r0, ct0 per iteration); I7 (norm bounds), I2 (constant-time rejection criterion); ADR-04, ADR-05; Lyubashevsky 2009 (Pa-7) — Fiat-Shamir with aborts, acceptance probability formula; CRYSTALS-Dilithium sec. 2 (Pa-4) — signing loop algorithms; FIPS 204 Algorithm 2 (S2).

File: pqc-sign/src/sign.rs

Signing loop pseudocode (no bodies — signatures only):

pub fn sign(sk: &SignSecretKey, msg: &[u8], params: &SignParams, rng: &mut dyn CryptoRng)
    -> Result<Signature, SignError>;

fn expand_mask(rho_prime: &Seed, kappa: u16, params: &SignParams) -> PolyVec; // y sampler
fn high_bits(r: &PolyVec, alpha: i32) -> PolyVec;   // w1 = HighBits(w, 2*gamma2)
fn low_bits(r: &PolyVec, alpha: i32) -> PolyVec;    // LowBits(w, 2*gamma2)
fn make_hint(z: &PolyVec, r: &PolyVec, alpha: i32) -> (PolyVec, usize); // h + popcount
fn norm_bound_check(v: &PolyVec, bound: i32) -> bool; // ||v||_inf <= bound; CT
fn sample_challenge(seed: &[u8; 32], tau: usize) -> Poly; // sparse +-1 challenge

Signing loop steps (per FIPS 204 Algorithm 2):

  1. Decode sk → (rho, K, tr, s1_hat, s2_hat, t0_hat) (NTT-domain secrets loaded once).
  2. Derive rho_prime = H(K || rnd || tr || mu) where mu = H(tr || msg).
  3. Loop with counter kappa = 0, 2*L, 4*L, …:
    • y = expand_mask(rho_prime, kappa, params) — L polys, coeffs in (-gamma1, gamma1].
    • y_hat = ntt(y).
    • w = intt(A_hat * y_hat) — matrix-vector in NTT domain.
    • w1 = high_bits(w, 2*gamma2).
    • c_tilde = H(mu || encode_w1(w1)) — 32-byte challenge seed.
    • c = sample_challenge(c_tilde, tau) — sparse +-1 poly (weight tau).
    • c_hat = ntt(c).
    • z = intt(y_hat + c_hat * s1_hat) — response vector.
    • Reject if norm_bound_check(z, gamma1 - beta) fails (I7).
    • r0 = low_bits(w - intt(c_hat * s2_hat), 2*gamma2).
    • Reject if norm_bound_check(r0, gamma2 - beta) fails.
    • ct0 = intt(c_hat * t0_hat).
    • (h, hint_count) = make_hint(-ct0, w - intt(c_hat*s2_hat) + ct0, 2*gamma2).
    • Reject if hint_count > omega (too many hints).
    • Return Signature { c_tilde, z, h }.
  4. kappa += 2*L each iteration; limit iterations to avoid infinite loop (return Err(SignError::MaxAttemptsExceeded) after 1000).
  5. Zeroize y, r0, ct0, intermediate polyvecs on drop (P8, I2).

NTT-domain caching: expand A_hat once from rho before the loop; cache s1_hat, s2_hat, t0_hat on key load. Observability hook: increment SIGN_REJECTION_COUNT atomic counter on each loop rejection (P10).

10.3 Deliverable

  • pqc_sign::sign compiles for ML-DSA-44/65/87 and produces Signature bytes matching FIPS 204 ACVP vectors for each parameter set.
  • pqc-kat passes all signing KAT vectors deterministically when seeded with ACVP RNG; pqc-cli sign subcommand functional.

Check Target

Hint count popcount(h) <= omega per parameter set

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug / Recovery

NTT-domain lifetime confusion Sign output off by ring element Accumulating c_hat * s1_hat without intt before norm check Enforce Domain tag; compile error if mixed

Gamma1 expansion range wrong Norm check always fails → infinite rejection Wrong parameter slot for expand_mask Assert max(y.coeffs) < gamma1; log first iteration

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Dominant cost: NTT matrix-vector A_hat * y_hat (L×K NTTs per loop iteration); cache A_hat before loop. Stack depth: PolyVec for y, w, z, r0, ct0 = 5 × L or K × 256 × 4 bytes — keep on stack, avoid heap allocation in loop.
  • Rejection rate: ~20–40 % iterations rejected; expected 4–7 iterations. Over-allocation of PolyVec buffers acceptable; reuse across iterations.
  • Hash throughput: H(mu || encode_w1(w1)) per iteration — minimize encode_w1 allocation by writing to a fixed-size scratch buffer.
  • No allocation inside loop: pre-allocate all PolyVec scratch outside loop and wipe/reuse; SIGN_REJECTION_COUNT.fetch_add is relaxed ordering — no cost.

10.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
All-zero sk; sign called with wrong param set Detect in decode / detect sk.params != params; return Err(InvalidKey) / Err(ParamMismatch) before loop
msg empty (0 bytes); msg > 2^32 bytes Both valid per FIPS 204: `mu = H(tr
kappa wraps u16 (> 65535); RNG failure (hedged mode) Saturate at max-attempts error before overflow; propagate Err(RngError) immediately, no fallback to deterministic
Hint computation negative r0 Centered representation; ensure make_hint uses centred LowBits

10.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: pqc_sign_rejection_total{param_set} counter (atomic u64, relaxed); pqc_sign_duration_us histogram per param set.
  • Logs: DEBUG rejection reason + iteration number (not secret values); WARN when iteration count > 20 (probable RNG or parameter bug). Never log y, z, s1, or the challenge seed.
  • CLI: pqc-cli sign accepts --hedged flag (default) or --deterministic; always zeroizes temp file.
  • Deployment: signing is stateless; no migration concern. Parameter set baked into SignSecretKey bytes.

Phase 11 — ML-DSA Verification

11.1 Objectives

  • Implement pqc_sign::verify for ML-DSA-44/65/87 per FIPS 204 Algorithm 3.
  • Recompute w1' via UseHint(h, A*z - c*t1*2^d) without access to s1, s2, t0.
  • Derive c' from H(tr, msg, w1') and compare to c_tilde from the signature byte-for-byte.
  • Reject on: c' != c_tilde, ||z||_inf exceeds bound, popcount(h) > omega, malformed encoding (I10); all rejection paths return Err(VerifyError::InvalidSignature) with no information about which check failed (P9).

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P2 (spec-exact verify), P9 (fail closed — unified VerifyError with no oracle), P1 (CT compare c' vs c_tilde); I6 (sign correctness), I7 (norm bound in verify), I10 (malformed rejection); ADR-28 (subtle CT for c' compare); Pa-10 (Ducas 2018) — UseHint definition; FIPS 204 Algorithm 3 (S2).

File: pqc-sign/src/verify.rs

pub fn verify(pk: &SignPublicKey, msg: &[u8], sig: &Signature, params: &SignParams)
    -> Result<(), VerifyError>;

fn use_hint(h: &PolyVec, r: &PolyVec, alpha: i32) -> PolyVec; // w1' recovery
fn decode_signature(bytes: &[u8], params: &SignParams) -> Result<Signature, VerifyError>;
fn decode_public_key(bytes: &[u8], params: &SignParams) -> Result<SignPublicKey, VerifyError>;

Verification steps (FIPS 204 Algorithm 3):

  1. Decode pk → (rho, t1). Decode sig → (c_tilde, z, h). Reject if any decode fails.
  2. Check ||z||_inf <= gamma1 - beta. Reject if not.
  3. Check popcount(h) <= omega (total across K polys). Reject if not.
  4. Derive mu = H(H(pk) || msg) using tr = H(pk) (precomputed in pk or recomputed).
  5. Expand A_hat from rho via XOF.
  6. Compute c = sample_challenge(c_tilde, tau) → sparse +-1 poly.
  7. z_hat = ntt(z).
  8. Az = intt(A_hat * z_hat) — K-element vector.
  9. ct1 = intt(ntt(c) * ntt(t1 * 2^d)) — scale t1 by 2^d (bit shift on coefficients) then NTT-multiply.
  10. w_approx = Az - ct1 — this equals w - c*s2 + c*t0 + c*e' approximately.
  11. w1' = use_hint(h, w_approx, 2*gamma2) — per coefficient (FIPS 204 sec. 6): if hint_bit == 1 round up to next alpha boundary, else keep high_bits(r).
  12. c' = H(mu || encode_w1(w1')).
  13. Accept iff ct_eq(c', c_tilde) is true (constant-time compare) (I2). tr is precomputed as H(pk) and stored in SignPublicKey.tr; recompute if absent.

11.3 Deliverable

  • pqc_sign::verify compiles for all three parameter sets; accepts all honestly signed messages and rejects tampered signature/message/key combos.
  • pqc-kat passes all FIPS 204 verify vectors byte-exact; pqc-cli verify subcommand functional, exits 0 on valid, 1 on invalid.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
FIPS 204 ACVP verify KAT — all 3 param sets 100 % pass
verify(pk, msg, sign(sk, msg)) == Ok 10 000 rounds per param set
Bit-flip in c_tilde, z, h, or msg (each independently) Reject
`
popcount(h) > omega Reject
Truncated signature bytes Err(InvalidSignature)
Timing variance on accept vs reject < 5 % relative std-dev (I2)

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • Dominant cost: A_hat * z_hat (L × K NTT-domain pointwise muls + K inverse NTTs). Same cost as one signing iteration.
  • A_hat expansion: re-expand from rho each verify call; cache option via SignPublicKey if caller retains key across many verify calls.
  • t1 scaling / UseHint: coefficient-wise left-shift by d bits before NTT (no mul needed, inlined); UseHint is purely integer arithmetic on 256 × K coefficients — both negligible vs. NTT.
  • encode_w1: fixed-size scratch buffer on stack (avoid heap allocation). Batch verify: not in scope for Phase 11 (single-message verify only); note as future extension.
  • Verification latency targets:
Param set Target (single core)
ML-DSA-44 < 200 µs
ML-DSA-65 < 300 µs
ML-DSA-87 < 400 µs

z coefficient = INT32_MIN Overflow in abs(); clamp before norm check; reject
