11.10 Operational Notes

  • Metrics: pqc_verify_total{result="ok"|"err", param_set} counter; pqc_verify_duration_us histogram per param set.
  • Logs: WARN on any VerifyError (do not log which check failed — avoids oracle); DEBUG param set, message length, signature size — no secret material.
  • CLI exit codes: 0 = valid, 1 = invalid signature, 2 = I/O or encoding error.
  • Stateless: verify holds no mutable state; safe to call from multiple threads on the same pk.

Phase 12 — Constant-Time Hardening

12.1 Objectives

  • Harden all secret-dependent paths across pqc-kem, pqc-sign, and pqc-ntt with primitives from pqc-ct: FO ciphertext comparison in decaps (I4), KEM implicit-rejection key selection, and signing loop intermediate scrubbing.
  • Implement pqc_ct::{ct_eq, ct_select, ct_cmov} using compiler barrier patterns (no unsafe branch on secret); implement Zeroize for all secret-key, masking, and intermediate types (P8).
  • Audit every if secret_cond branch across the codebase and replace with CT primitives. Target: no data-dependent branch or memory-access on secret values (I2).

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P1 (constant-time on all secret paths), P8 (zeroize on drop); I2 (timing independence gate), I4 (implicit rejection via ct_select); ADR-04 (subtle crate), ADR-05 (zeroize crate); Pa-11 (Kocher 1996) — timing attacks motivate every CT primitive here; Pa-12 (Bernstein 2005) — table-index leakage motivates no-branch CBD; Pa-14 (Bernstein & Yang 2019) — divstep barrier pattern for ct_eq.

File: pqc-ct/src/lib.rs

pub fn ct_eq_u32(a: u32, b: u32) -> u32;      // 0xFFFF_FFFF if equal, 0 otherwise; branch-free
pub fn ct_eq_bytes(a: &[u8], b: &[u8]) -> u8; // 1 if equal, 0 if not; no short-circuit
pub fn ct_select_u32(a: u32, b: u32, mask: u32) -> u32; // b if mask=0xFF.., a if mask=0
pub fn ct_cmov(dst: &mut [u8], src: &[u8], mask: u8);   // write src into dst if mask=0xFF..; branch-free
pub fn secure_zero(buf: &mut [u8]);           // volatile-write zero; not elided by compiler

Barrier pattern for ct_eq_bytes: XOR all bytes accumulating into a u8, no early exit, memory fence before return; use core::sync::atomic::compiler_fence(SeqCst) to prevent reordering; mark #[inline(never)] to resist LTO elimination.

FO comparison in pqc-kem/src/kem.rs (Phase 8 hardening): ct_decaps(sk, ct, params) -> SharedSecret decrypts m', re-encrypts to ct', computes mask = ct_eq_bytes(ct, ct'), then ct_selects between K_good and PRF(z, ct) using mask — no branch on mask value.

Implicit rejection selection (constant-time): K_good = G(m').0 from the re-encryption path; K_bad = PRF(z, ct) always computed (not conditionally); mask = ct_eq_bytes(&ct_bytes, &ct_prime_bytes) (0xFF or 0x00); result = ct_cmov(K_bad, K_good, mask) — one result, no branch.

Signing intermediates zeroization: PolyVec (y, r0, ct0) and Seed (rho_prime, m) implement ZeroizeOnDrop via Drop + secure_zero; SignSecretKey inner fields (s1, s2, t0) and KemSecretKey inner (m, dk_pke) likewise.

Audit checklist (recorded in docs/ct-audit.md):

Location Secret Original form Hardened form
kem/decaps FO compare + key select ciphertext / shared secret if ct == ct'; branch on match ct_eq_bytes + ct_cmov; ct_select with mask
sign/sign rejection check ` z
sign/sign y/r0 on drop masking vector implicit ZeroizeOnDrop
ntt butterfly / sample/cbd twiddle lookup / noise output public table index; XOR of PRF stream public, no hardening needed; secret output, accumulate CT
ct/ct_eq_bytes comparison byte XOR accumulate compiler_fence; #[inline(never)]

Variable-time exceptions (documented): expand_A (public matrix from public seed, P7); pqc-kat (test infrastructure only); NTT twiddle table indexing (indices are public, determined by algorithm structure not keys).

12.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-ct crate compiles with all primitives; decaps FO comparison and key-select path are constant-time (verified by timing test); all ZeroizeOnDrop implementations wipe secret fields.
  • docs/ct-audit.md lists every secret-touching path and its hardening status; cargo test passes across all crates.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Target
ct_eq_bytes([0;32],[0;32])==1; ct_eq_bytes([0;32],[1;32])==0 Both pass
Timing: decaps valid vs tampered ciphertext Relative std-dev < 5 % over 100 000 trials
Zeroize: secret fields set to 0 after drop Verified via read_volatile in test
Audit document covers all secret paths All rows filled in ct-audit.md
MIRI on ct primitives; ct_eq_bytes compiler output No UB detected; objdump shows no conditional jump on input
KAT still passing after hardening All Phase 13 ACVP vectors still byte-exact

12.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
fixtures/ct_eq_vectors.json 32-byte pairs with expected eq/neq Matches 1/0
fixtures/decaps_tampered_ct.bin 1-byte tampered ciphertext Same-length SharedSecret; not distinguishable from valid
fixtures/decaps_valid_ct.bin Valid ciphertext Byte-exact SharedSecret (matches ACVP)
fixtures/zeroize_canary.bin Write known pattern; drop key; read All-zero
fixtures/ct_timing_series.json 10 000 (valid, tampered) decaps times Statistical near-equality

12.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug / Recovery
LTO eliminates ct_eq_bytes comparison Branch appears in optimized binary Inlining collapses XOR to short-circuit Mark #[inline(never)]; add black_box on inputs
compiler_fence insufficient for CPU ordering Timing leak on OOO processors SeqCst fence is compile-time only Add atomic::fence(SeqCst) (hardware fence)
secure_zero optimized away Secret remains in freed memory Compiler removes dead stores Use write_volatile; test with sanitizer
K_bad path not always computed Branch introduced by if mask wrapper Caller uses result before both paths computed Compute K_bad unconditionally, then select
Zeroize on PolyVec iterates polys One poly not zeroized if panics mid-drop Partial drop Use flat [i32; N] backing + single secure_zero call
ct_select with wrong mask width Incorrect key returned silently u32 mask applied to u8 slice Parameterize by byte mask; unit-test both mask values
Secret branch in rejection norm check z norm check leaks z magnitude `

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • ct_eq_bytes: 32-byte fixed-length comparison; ~8 ns; negligible vs. NTT. compiler_fence/atomic::fence: one hardware fence per decaps; negligible.
  • K_bad computation: PRF(z, ct) always runs — adds ~1 µs; necessary cost of I4.
  • ZeroizeOnDrop: secure_zero on PolyVec (256 × L × 4 bytes = 4 KB max); ~200 ns; on hot path but unavoidable (P8).
  • No branching penalty from CT primitives in normal operation (all paths execute unconditionally). LLVM black_box: wrap inputs to ct_eq_bytes to prevent constant-folding in test builds.

12.8 Edge Cases

Case Handling
ct_eq_bytes called with different-length slices Panic with length mismatch; caller must enforce equal lengths before calling
Zeroize called on already-zero buffer; multiple drops (double-free scenario) No-op, write_volatile still executes; secure_zero idempotent, no UB from zeroing twice
ct_select with mask = 0x80 (partial) Not a valid mask; only 0x00 or 0xFF accepted; assert in debug
Drop during unwind (panic in sign loop) ZeroizeOnDrop still fires via Drop; secrets wiped
Secret in a cloned type Clone on SignSecretKey copies secret bytes; document as sensitive; zeroize on drop of clone
Timing test on virtual machine VM scheduling jitter can inflate variance; note CI environment requirement

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
CT comparison optimized out Timing oracle → distinguishing attack on FO KEM security break (CCA2 → CPA) CI objdump check; LLVM IR audit
secure_zero elided / zeroize missing field Memory forensic leaks SK material Key compromise / partial SK exposure CI sanitizer test on every merge; audit all secret types in ct-audit.md
K_bad not computed Timing distinguisher on decaps failure Implicit rejection broken (I4) Code review: assert unconditional PRF call
Fence missing Out-of-order execution side-channel Subtle timing leak (microarchitectural) Add hardware fence; note platform dependency

12.10 Operational Notes

  • Metric: no CT-specific metrics (leaking timing info); document absence as intentional.
  • Audit trail: docs/ct-audit.md is a required deliverable; reviewed on every PR touching pqc-kem or pqc-sign.
  • CI gate: cargo test --release + RUSTFLAGS="-C opt-level=3" timing test must pass; objdump check for conditional jumps in ct_eq_bytes; MIRI (cargo miri test -p pqc-ct) must complete with zero UB reports.
  • Sanitizers: cargo test -p pqc-kem -p pqc-sign under AddressSanitizer + MemorySanitizer in CI.
  • Deployment note: CT guarantees hold on the specific CPU micro-arch tested; document in release notes if new hardware introduces new attack surfaces (e.g., LVI, MDS). Phase 14 adds formal timing-variance testing and side-channel review; Phase 12 lays the primitive foundation.

Phase 13 — KAT conformance + parameter sets + API

13.1 Objectives

  • Drive all six parameter sets (ML-KEM-512/768/1024, ML-DSA-44/65/87) through FIPS 203/204 ACVP known-answer vectors.
  • Assert byte-exact outputs: public keys, ciphertexts, shared secrets, signatures, seed-derived intermediates.
  • Wire a deterministic-RNG (DRBG) hook that reproduces KAT seeds so each test vector is fully reproducible.
  • Finalize the public keygen/encaps/decaps/sign/verify API surface for each parameter set; lock names, types, error variants.
  • Constraint: no secret-dependent branch is added during API finalization; I1 and I2 hold.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P2 (byte-exact ACVP conformance), P11 (algorithm traceability to FIPS sections); I1 (KAT gate); ADR-13 (FIPS ACVP vectors), ADR-20 (reproducible builds); FIPS 203 ACVP (S7), NIST ACVP protocol (S7); R7 (NIST ACVP Server — JSON schema reference).

pqc-kat crate — vector loading

File Purpose
kat/fips203_kat.rs Parse FIPS 203 ACVP JSON: seed → (pk, sk, ct, ss) per parameter set
kat/fips204_kat.rs Parse FIPS 204 ACVP JSON: seed → (pk, sk, msg, sig) per parameter set
kat/drbg_hook.rs DeterministicRng wrapper: NIST-style counter-mode or raw-byte-feed; injectable into keygen/encaps/sign
  • KAT driver: iterate each vector, inject seed bytes into DeterministicRng, call crate API, compare every output field with ct_eq_bytes; report mismatch as KatMismatch { param_set, vector_index, field, expected_hex, got_hex }.
  • Use serde_json + hex to parse NIST ACVP JSON format; fixture files embedded via include_bytes! for no_std portability.

Parameter-set dispatch

pub enum KemParamSet { MlKem512, MlKem768, MlKem1024 }
pub enum SignParamSet { MlDsa44, MlDsa65, MlDsa87 }

pub fn kem_keygen(params: KemParamSet, rng: &mut impl CryptoRng) -> (KemPublicKey, KemSecretKey);
pub fn kem_encaps(params: KemParamSet, pk: &KemPublicKey, rng: &mut impl CryptoRng) -> (Ciphertext, SharedSecret);
pub fn kem_decaps(params: KemParamSet, sk: &KemSecretKey, ct: &Ciphertext) -> SharedSecret;
pub fn sign_keygen(params: SignParamSet, rng: &mut impl CryptoRng) -> (SignPublicKey, SignSecretKey);
pub fn dsa_sign(params: SignParamSet, sk: &SignSecretKey, msg: &[u8], rng: &mut impl CryptoRng) -> Signature;
pub fn dsa_verify(params: SignParamSet, pk: &SignPublicKey, msg: &[u8], sig: &Signature) -> Result<(), PqcError>;
  • Dispatch via match params into generic internal functions parameterized by KemParams / SignParams constants.
  • pqc-core exposes KemParams::for_level(KemParamSet) and SignParams::for_level(SignParamSet) returning the locked constant structs.

API hardening

  • All public-key and ciphertext inputs validated for exact byte length before any decoding; return PqcError::InvalidLength otherwise (I10).
  • SharedSecret zeroizes on drop (I2, P8).
  • Signature and secret keys implement ZeroizeOnDrop.

13.3 Deliverable

  • pqc-kat crate compiles and runs cargo test --release -p pqc-kat producing pass/fail per vector.
  • pqc-cli kat subcommand prints per-parameter-set PASS/FAIL with vector count.
  • Public API in pqc-kem and pqc-sign stabilized with #[deny(missing_docs)]; all six parameter sets operational end-to-end.
  • DeterministicRng documented and exported for downstream conformance testing.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
KAT pass rate 100 % across all FIPS 203/204 ACVP vectors for all six parameter sets
Byte-exact match public key, ciphertext, shared secret, signature — each field
API symbol stability zero #[deprecated] warnings; no renamed public symbols after this phase
Compile (no_std) pqc-kat compiles with default-features = false
Error coverage every PqcError variant exercised by at least one test
cargo clippy zero warnings at --deny warnings

File Vectors Purpose

kat/data/fips203_mlkem768.json NIST ACVP set ML-KEM-768

kat/data/wrong_length_pk.bin hand-crafted off-by-one byte key; InvalidLength expected
  • Regression: re-run all fixtures on every CI push; any mismatch blocks merge.
  • drbg_hook fixture: known seed 00..00 produces the first ACVP vector's outputs.

13.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
DRBG byte-order mismatch KAT fails for first byte of pk Big/little-endian seed feed differs from NIST convention Compare raw seed byte stream to ACVP test-case seed field
Off-by-one in parameter constants Ciphertext length wrong by 32 bytes du/dv bits looked up from wrong row in table assert_eq!(Ciphertext::len(params), expected) before encode
t vs t1 packing confusion pk KAT mismatch Public key encodes full t instead of t1 (high bits only) Diff intermediate t1 bytes against FIPS 203 sec. 5.1
Signature c_tilde truncation sig KAT mismatch Hash output truncated to wrong length (32 vs 48 bytes for ML-DSA-87) Log c_tilde hex and compare field by field
DeterministicRng re-seeding subsequent vectors use wrong seed Rng state not reset between vectors Add rng.reset(seed) assertion; check call count
JSON field name casing parse fails silently ACVP uses pk vs publicKey inconsistently across suites Use #[serde(alias = "...")] and add smoke-test for field presence

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • KAT suite is CI-only; runtime is not latency-critical, but all-vectors run must complete in < 60 s on a 2-core CI runner.
  • include_bytes! embeds fixture JSON into the binary; total fixture size ~ 15 MB across six sets — use lazy_static to decode once per test session.
  • Parameter dispatch match is O(1); no dynamic dispatch overhead in API hot path.
  • DeterministicRng must produce bytes at the same rate as keygen/sign consume them; allocate a pre-filled buffer to avoid per-byte overhead.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty message for DSA sign: valid (zero-length msg slice); verify must accept same zero-length msg.
  • Re-use of a KAT seed across parameter sets: each test must reset DeterministicRng fully before each vector.
  • Parameter set boundary: ML-KEM-512 uses k=2, matrix is 2×2; ensure PolyMat indexing does not access row 2.
  • kem_decaps called with sk from a different parameter set than ct: must return PqcError::InvalidLength or domain error before any arithmetic.
  • Signature with maximum hint-bit count exactly at the limit: verify must accept; one over the limit must reject.
  • sign called with deterministic rng that exhausts its byte stream mid-rejection loop: surface PqcError::RngExhausted, not a panic.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
KAT vector mismatch CI test failure with hex diff Single parameter set; others may still pass Bisect: isolate phase (keygen/encaps/sign) then intermediate field
Missing ACVP fixture file include_bytes! compile error Build fails entirely Restore from kat/data/ in git; verify file checksums
API symbol renamed post-publish downstream crate breakage All consumers of pqc-kem/pqc-sign Semantic versioning; add #[deprecated(since="...", note="...")] for one cycle
DeterministicRng state corruption random KAT failures across runs Non-deterministic; hard to reproduce Add rng.call_count() assertion per vector; log seed + call index

13.10 Operational Notes

  • CI job: cargo test -p pqc-kat --release pinned to the ACVP fixture commit hash; fixture updates require explicit PR.
  • Metrics: count of passing/failing vectors per parameter set emitted as structured log lines (tracing::info!); parseable by CI log scanner.
  • pqc-cli kat --json emits machine-readable result array for integration with NIST ACVP reporting tools.
  • No secret material in KAT logs; log only parameter set name, vector index, field name, expected/got hex for mismatches.
  • API documentation: every public symbol has a doc comment with parameter-set example and error-variant list; enforced by #[deny(missing_docs)].

Phase 14 — hardening + fuzz + perf + side-channel review + packing

14.1 Objectives

  • Fuzz all entry points with malformed ciphertexts, signatures, public keys; assert no panic, no memory unsafety, no secret leak. Verify implicit-rejection indistinguishability: a tampered ciphertext decapsulates to a deterministic PRF-derived key, not a distinguishable error.
  • Timing-variance test on all secret-dependent paths, quantifying variance within an acceptable noise floor; benchmark NTT, keygen, sign, verify across all parameter sets and establish/enforce performance baselines.
  • Documented side-channel review covering constant-time audit, zeroization, and compiler optimization risks; publish-ready crate packing (docs, README, CHANGELOG, license, #![no_std] option where applicable).

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Applies: P9 (fail closed on malformed), P1 (CT audit final confirmation), P10 (observability without leakage); I2 (timing test), I4 (implicit rejection), I10 (fuzz gate); ADR-15 (cargo-fuzz + afl), ADR-16 (criterion), ADR-23 (dudect); Pa-11 (Kocher 1996) — timing variance test methodology; Pa-13 (Becker 2023) — masked Kyber decaps cost model for Phase 12/14 scope.

Fuzzing (pqc-kat + standalone fuzz targets): fuzz_kem_decaps.rs (random bytes → Ciphertext → kem_decaps; no panic, 32-byte output) and fuzz_sign_verify.rs (random bytes → Signature → dsa_verify; Ok or typed Err, no panic); fuzz_pk_decode.rs (random bytes into KemPublicKey/SignPublicKey::from_bytes; no panic) and fuzz_encode_roundtrip.rs (random Poly coefficients clamped to [0,q) → encode → decode; roundtrip equality). Use cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) plus honggfuzz for coverage diversity; corpus seeds are all ACVP ciphertext/signature fixtures plus single-bit-flip variants from kat/gen_malformed.py.

Implicit-rejection test: test_implicit_rejection(params, sk, valid_ct, encaps_ss) bit-flips each byte of valid_ct and asserts the decapsulated key differs from encaps_ss, is constant across identical tampered inputs, and that kem_decaps never returns Err(...) (I4) — its signature returns SharedSecret, not Result<SharedSecret>.

Timing-variance test (pqc-ct): criterion with black_box and high iteration counts measures wall-clock ns distribution for kem_decaps (valid vs tampered ciphertext, must be statistically indistinguishable) and ct_eq (equal vs unequal SharedSecret, must be constant-time); also run under valgrind --tool=memcheck and cargo-careful for UB detection, with optional ctgrind/dudect bindings for leakage-resilience testing.

Benchmarks (benches/):

Benchmark Params Target (ns, single core, release)
ntt_forward / ntt_inverse 256-coeff, q=3329 < 2 000 (both)
kem_keygen / kem_encaps ML-KEM-768 < 100 000 (both)
kem_decaps ML-KEM-768 < 120 000
sign_keygen ML-DSA-65 < 150 000
dsa_sign ML-DSA-65 < 400 000 (avg, incl. retries)
dsa_verify ML-DSA-65 < 200 000

Criterion report saved to target/criterion/; CI fails if any benchmark regresses > 20 % from baseline (M1–M9).

Side-channel review (SIDE_CHANNEL_REVIEW.md):

Path Secret Mitigation Status
FO ciphertext comparison / KEM key select (kem_decaps) re-encrypted ct vs input ct; comparison result ct_eq_bytes; ct_select (conditional move) Required: I2, I4, P1
CBD sampling loop / compiler barrier noise values; prevent dead-store elision fixed-iteration loop, no early exit; core::hint::black_box on zeroize targets Required: P7
Dilithium rejection loop iteration count depends on secret norms loop count is public (sign may retry); inner ops constant-time Acceptable: P4
t0 / s1 / s2 arithmetic secret key NTT ops fully data-independent Required
Stack zeroization y, m, intermediate seeds ZeroizeOnDrop on all secret locals Required: P8

Crate packing: Cargo.toml per crate sets edition = "2021", license = "MIT OR Apache-2.0", documentation, repository, keywords; #![no_std] for pqc-core/poly/ntt/ct/encode (with alloc feature for Vec-returning APIs), pqc-hash conditionally no_std+alloc, pqc-kem/pqc-sign/pqc-kat/pqc-cli require std. README.md per crate: one-paragraph purpose, signature-only usage snippet, FIPS reference, security notice; CHANGELOG.md at workspace root with a ## [Unreleased] section covering all Phase 1–14 items.

14.3 Deliverable

  • Fuzz targets compile and run cargo fuzz run fuzz_kem_decaps -- -max_total_time=300 without crash or panic; implicit-rejection test at 100 % pass rate across all parameter sets and single-byte-flip positions.
  • Criterion benchmark report for all eight benchmark targets; SIDE_CHANNEL_REVIEW.md committed with every row Mitigated or Accepted-with-rationale.
  • cargo publish --dry-run succeeds for all eleven crates (no missing metadata); #[deny(missing_docs)] across public API crates, cargo doc --no-deps produces complete HTML without warnings.

14.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal
Compiler eliminates ct_eq Timing test shows bimodal distribution Dead-store elimination or branch speculation Inspect LLVM IR; add black_box; verify with objdump -d
Implicit-rejection key collides with honest key Assertion fails spuriously Birthday paradox at 32 bytes is negligible; but PRF(z,c) must differ from K Log both keys as hex; verify z is the rejection seed, not a shared-secret seed
Fuzz harness panics in unwrap on encode input Fuzz target counts as crash Debug assertions in encode path Replace unwrap with ?; fuzz targets must use Result-returning wrappers
Benchmark variance due to OS scheduling Criterion reports high variance CI runner shares CPU Use criterion.configure_from_args(); pin to one CPU with taskset in CI
no_std breakage from transitive std import thumbv7em build fails A dependency added std feature implicitly cargo tree --features "..." to locate; add default-features = false
Zeroize elision on release build Secrets remain in stack memory Compiler optimizes out stores to memory it deems dead Use zeroize crate's volatile_write; test with AddressSanitizer heap dump
cargo publish ordering failure dependency not yet published Workspace crates must be published in dependency order Script publishes pqc-core → poly → ntt → hash → ct → sample → encode → kem → sign → kat → cli

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • NTT is the bottleneck for keygen/encaps/sign; ensure zeta table is static and cache-line-aligned (64-byte). Rejection sampling in dsa_sign runs on average 4–7 iterations; inner A*y NTT multiply dominates; profile with perf stat to confirm.
  • Fuzz binary is debug-mode by default; use cargo fuzz run --release to avoid misleading performance signals in timing tests.
  • Criterion: use iter_batched with SmallInput to avoid allocator noise, pre-generating keys outside the measured loop; cargo doc can be slow on first run (~30 s) due to Keccak cross-links, not a production concern.
  • no_std crates: avoid heap allocation in hot paths; all polynomial operations on stack or caller-provided buffers.

14.8 Edge Cases

  • Fuzz input of exactly 0 bytes to kem_decaps must return PqcError::InvalidLength, not index out of bounds; an all-zero ciphertext is syntactically valid length but semantically invalid, and implicit rejection must fire.
  • All-0xFF public key: byte-length valid, arithmetic reduction will produce values; must not panic on out-of-range coefficients after decode.
  • dsa_sign with a message of 2^32 bytes: ensure msg length does not overflow u32 in SHAKE domain separator construction.
  • DeterministicRng byte-exhaustion mid-loop during fuzzing returns PqcError::RngExhausted, not a panic or infinite loop.
  • Benchmark on a machine with q mod word_size == 0: Barrett precomputed constant must handle edge values without overflow.
  • Crate publish with pre-existing version on crates.io: --dry-run must not fail; actual publish requires version bump.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Fuzz crash discovered libFuzzer crash artifact in fuzz/artifacts/ Security-critical; potential panic in public API Minimize artifact, identify path via RUST_BACKTRACE=1, fix then re-fuzz
Timing oracle found by dudect t-statistic > 4.5 on secret path CCA2 security broken for affected operation Audit IR of the failing path; add black_box or rewrite with ct_select; re-test
Benchmark regression > 20 % CI benchmark step fails Performance SLA missed; not a security issue Profile with cargo flamegraph; identify culprit commit via git bisect
no_std breakage post-merge thumbv7em CI target fails Embedded users blocked Revert offending dependency update; add no_std build to PR gate
Side-channel audit item not mitigated / cargo publish out-of-order Review row left open / dependency version not found Potential information leak / consumers cannot install Block publish, assign owner, resolve before cargo publish; publish script enforces topological order

14.10 Operational Notes

  • CI pipeline final stage: cargo fuzz run --release fuzz_kem_decaps -- -max_total_time=300 -jobs=2; artifact upload on failure.
  • Security advisory process: if a fuzz crash reveals a vulnerability, embargo via security@<org>, publish advisory with CVSS score before patch release.
  • SIDE_CHANNEL_REVIEW.md is a living document, re-reviewed on every Rust toolchain update (new optimizer passes may re-introduce eliminated barriers); benchmarks committed to benches/baseline.json via cargo criterion --message-format=json, with critcmp for regression detection.
  • pqc-cli ships a --no-color flag and structured JSON output for scripting; no secret material in stdout or log output.
  • Crate versioning: 0.1.0 for initial publish, all eleven crates in lockstep (workspace.version); breaking API changes require a major bump. Documentation links to FIPS 203/204 PDFs in top-level README.md with a security notice that production use requires an independent security audit.

Threat Model

Trust Boundaries

The architecture diagram defines four boundary zones:

  • Zone A (untrusted input): ciphertexts, public keys, signatures, KAT vectors arriving from the network or filesystem.
  • Zone B (semi-trusted): seed bytes, randomness from the OS CSPRNG; assumed uniform but must not be trusted for secrecy beyond zeroization.
  • Zone C (trusted computation): pqc-kem, pqc-sign, pqc-ntt, pqc-poly, pqc-sample, pqc-encode, pqc-ct, pqc-hash; correctness guaranteed by KAT (I1).
  • Zone D (operator): pqc-cli; commands arrive from a shell; secrets in env vars or files are operator responsibility.

Threat Table

# Threat Surface Attack Vector Impact Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 Malformed ciphertext input pqc-kem::decaps Tampered u/v bytes, wrong length Panic / OOB read Strict length checks before decode; I10: reject without panic Unit tests; fuzz via Phase 14 decaps_reject_total counter Return Err(InvalidCiphertext)
T2 Malformed public key pqc-kem::encaps, pqc-sign::verify Wrong byte count, out-of-range coefficients Incorrect polynomial, silent bad output Deserialize checks coefficient range [0,q); reject on length mismatch KAT negative-case vectors key_parse_error_total Return Err(InvalidPublicKey)
T3 Malformed signature pqc-sign::verify Oversized z, excess hint bits, bad c~ False accept / panic ` z
T4 Timing side-channel on FO comparison pqc-kem::decaps Measure decapsulation latency across tampered ciphertexts Key recovery (Bleichenbacher-style) pqc-ct::ct_eq for ciphertext comparison; no early exit (I2, P1, P3) Timing variance test in Phase 14 None (must not emit timing signals) Enforce via CI timing gate
T5 Timing side-channel on norm check pqc-sign::sign Time rejection-loop iteration count variations Leak of s1/s2 partial info Constant-time coefficient extraction; do not branch on secret coefficients Phase 12 + Phase 14 audit sign_rejection_iterations histogram (aggregate only, no per-secret values; P10) Audit + redesign if leak confirmed
T6 Secrets remaining in memory All secret-key paths Process memory dump, cold-boot Full key compromise pqc-ct::zeroize on Drop for s1,s2,t0,y,m,SharedSecret (P8, Phase 12) ASAN / Valgrind in CI — Enforce ZeroizeOnDrop trait bounds at compile time
T7 Weak randomness at keygen/encaps pqc-kem::keygen, pqc-sign::keygen RNG bias or reuse Lattice attack recovers secret Delegate to OS CSPRNG (getrandom crate); never accept caller-provided seeds in production API RNG-failure error propagation rng_error_total Hard fail; no silent fallback
T8 Parameter-set confusion All APIs Pass ML-DSA-44 key to ML-DSA-87 operation Incorrect security level; potential panic KemParams / SignParams carry security level; operations assert matching params Type-level enforcement where possible; runtime assert otherwise — Reject with Err(ParamMismatch)
T9 Integer overflow in reduction pqc-core::Zq, pqc-ntt Carefully crafted coefficient stream I8 violation; corrupted polynomial Montgomery/Barrett intermediates proven to fit i64; i32 storage reduced before use; I8 Property tests in Phase 1 — Panic in debug; saturating assert in release
T10 NTT domain misuse pqc-poly, pqc-ntt Multiply two Normal-domain polys via NTT path Silent incorrect result Domain enum tag; operations assert or enforce via type; P6 Compile-time (type system) + runtime assert — Type-level rejection
T11 Denial of service via signing loop pqc-sign::sign Trigger adversarial RNG (not attacker-controlled, but validate) CPU exhaustion from excessive rejections Expected rejection count per FIPS 204 Table 1; abort with error after N_MAX iterations Rejection counter; alert if > 2× expected mean sign_rejection_total Return Err(SigningFailed) after limit
T12 Supply-chain: dependency compromise All crates Malicious crate update (sha3, getrandom, zeroize) Broken KATs / timing leak / secret exfil Pin exact dependency versions in Cargo.lock; audit Cargo.lock in CI; minimal dep set KAT failure in CI CI KAT pass/fail Revert dep; re-audit
T13 State persistence / key reuse pqc-cli key files Reused ephemeral key or nonce Security reduction ML-KEM encaps samples fresh m per call; ML-DSA sign samples fresh y per call; no state file for ephemerals Code audit (no persisted nonce state) — Document key lifecycle; purge old key files
T14 Operator key file exposure pqc-cli World-readable key file written to disk Secret key leak Emit warning if file permissions > 0o600; document secure storage CLI startup permission check key_file_permission_warn_total chmod; rotate keys

Failure Modes & Recovery

Failure Taxonomy

Class Symptoms Root Causes Detection Auto Recovery Manual Recovery Operator Actions Forensic Artifacts Long-Term Mitigation
KAT mismatch ACVP vector test returns FAIL; byte comparison differs Off-by-one in bit-packing; wrong twiddle zeta; wrong reduce order Phase 13 KAT harness; CI gate None; deterministic failure Bisect by parameter set and phase; diff against reference implementation Halt release; report to NIST ACVP portal KAT log with first-diverging vector index and byte offset Comprehensive per-section unit tests; invariant I1
NTT identity failure inverse_ntt(forward_ntt(p)) != p; ntt_mul != schoolbook_mul Wrong root-of-unity ordering; missing Montgomery normalization factor Phase 3 property tests; diff-test against pqc-poly schoolbook None Audit zeta table generation; check normalization 1/256 in INTT Pin correct zeta table; re-run full KAT Failing polynomial hex dump Reference zeta table locked in const; I5
Decryption failure (PKE/KEM) decaps returns wrong shared secret (non-negligible rate) Du/dv compression parameters wrong; noise bounds too tight for decompression error Phase 7 correctness test; correctness test over 10^6 random keypairs None (deterministic failure class) Re-check compress/decompress rounding; validate FIPS 203 sec. 4.2 Audit parameter set constants against FIPS 203 Table 1 Failing (pk,sk,ct,ss) tuple I3; Phase 7 correctness test in CI
FO implicit rejection failure Tampered ciphertext returns same shared secret as valid one ct comparison non-constant-time causing early exit with correct key; z seed mismatch Phase 8 unit test: tampered ct must return PRF(z,c), not K None Audit pqc-ct::ct_eq usage; confirm z is in secret key Fix FO code path; re-run timing test ct, sk, returned-ss triple I4; P3; constant-time CI gate
Signature verification false-negative verify returns false for a honestly generated signature Hint application wrong (UseHint); high-bit decomposition mismatch; wrong challenge hash Phase 11 unit test; KAT Phase 13 None Audit UseHint / HighBits / LowBits against FIPS 204 sec. 7 Re-derive decomposition helpers from spec (pk, msg, sig) failing tuple I6; Phase 11 test in CI
Signature norm bound violation sign outputs signature later rejected by verify; ` z ` exceeds bound Rejection sampling threshold wrong; premature loop termination Phase 10 unit test on z-norm; Phase 11 round-trip Rejection loop retries (up to N_MAX); P4
Encoding roundtrip error decode(encode(p)) != p; payload corruption Wrong bit-width; endianness; off-by-one in BitPack Phase 6 roundtrip property tests None Bisect to polynomial-element level; compare with FIPS byte layout appendix Audit packing layout against FIPS serialization spec Failing poly hex pairs I9; property test each codec independently
Integer overflow / range violation Panic in debug build; wrong result in release; I8 violated Missing reduction after add; intermediate exceeding i32 range Phase 1 range property tests; debug-mode overflow panics Debug: panic; release: reduction assert Add explicit reduce step; widen intermediate to i64 where needed Audit arithmetic hot paths in Phase 3 and Phase 7 Coefficient value at fault site I8; reduce-after-each-op discipline
Constant-time violation Timing variance correlates with secret in statistical test Branch on secret coefficient or FO comparison result Phase 14 timing variance test; cargo careful; dudect None Replace branch with pqc-ct::ct_select / cmov Audit flagged paths; rewrite; re-test Timing trace data (latency distribution) I2; P1; Phase 12 full audit
Memory leak of secret Secret key bytes found in freed allocation (Valgrind, sanitizer) Missing zeroize on Drop; early return before zeroize ASAN/Valgrind in CI; cargo test --features zeroize-checks None Add ZeroizeOnDrop derive; audit each return path Add drop_in_place guard; rotate affected keys Sanitizer report P8; Phase 12 zeroization pass
Invalid parameter set at runtime ParamMismatch error; incorrect security level silently used Wrong constant selected; missing level check at API boundary Runtime parameter assertion; unit tests per param set Return Err(ParamMismatch) Select correct KemParams/SignParams variant Validate key provenance; re-generate if unknown Error log with expected vs actual params Typed parameter enforcement; compile-time generics
RNG failure Err(RngError) at keygen / encaps / sign OS entropy unavailable at boot; /dev/urandom not seeded getrandom error propagation None; hard fail (no fallback) Wait for OS entropy; retry Ensure entropy source is available before invoking crypto System entropy pool status Propagate error; never silently degrade to weak RNG
Panic from malformed input Process crash; DOS via untrusted input OOB slice index; unwrap on bad parse Fuzz corpus in Phase 14 None Fix parse site; add bounds check Redeploy patched binary Fuzz crash artifact (.corpus file) I10; P9; no unwrap on external data
ACVP vector drift KAT passes locally but fails on NIST update FIPS errata changing encoding or constant Pin ACVP vector files in repo by hash; CI diff on update None Re-audit changed sections; update implementation Track FIPS errata; subscribe to NIST updates Diff of ACVP JSON files Lock KAT vector files in repo; review on any NIST update

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

Metric Type Labels Purpose
pqc_kat_pass_total Counter suite={kem,dsa}, param_set KAT conformance monitoring
pqc_kat_fail_total Counter suite, param_set, vector_id Regression detection
pqc_keygen_duration_ns Histogram scheme, param_set Performance baseline
pqc_encaps_duration_ns Histogram param_set Performance baseline
pqc_decaps_duration_ns Histogram param_set FO overhead tracking
pqc_sign_duration_ns Histogram param_set Signing throughput
pqc_verify_duration_ns Histogram param_set Verification throughput
pqc_sign_rejection_iterations Histogram param_set Dilithium abort loop health; spike = anomaly
pqc_decaps_reject_total Counter param_set Invalid ciphertext rate
pqc_sig_verify_reject_total Counter param_set Invalid signature rate
pqc_key_parse_error_total Counter scheme, param_set Malformed key rate
pqc_rng_error_total Counter — RNG unavailability
pqc_key_file_permission_warn_total Counter — Operator hygiene
pqc_ntt_roundtrip_failures Counter — NTT regression in CI

Tracing Strategy

  • Spans: keygen, encaps, decaps, sign, verify — each carries scheme and param_set attributes.
  • Span attributes: operation success/failure, rejection count for sign, input byte length for parse operations.
  • No span attribute may carry a secret value, coefficient, shared secret, or private key fragment (P10).
  • Propagate trace_id from CLI invocation into each crate boundary; child spans created per major sub-operation (NTT, sample, encode).
  • Format: OpenTelemetry OTLP or structured JSON to stderr.

Structured Logging

Level Event Fields (no secrets)
INFO keygen_complete scheme, param_set, duration_ms
INFO encaps_complete param_set, duration_ms
INFO decaps_complete param_set, duration_ms, valid=true/false
INFO sign_complete param_set, duration_ms, rejection_count
INFO verify_complete param_set, duration_ms, accepted=true/false
WARN sign_high_rejection param_set, rejection_count, threshold
WARN key_file_permission path, actual_mode, expected_mode
ERROR kat_vector_mismatch suite, param_set, vector_id, field (no raw bytes)
ERROR rng_failure os_error
ERROR malformed_input_rejected operation, error_kind

All log events: ISO-8601 timestamp, crate, level, JSON body. Log sink: stderr by default; configurable to file via CLI flag.

Dashboard Design

Panel Visualization Alert Threshold
KAT pass/fail by param set Status tiles (green/red) Any fail → P0
keygen/encaps/decaps latency p50/p99 Time series p99 > 2× baseline → WARN
sign latency + rejection histogram Heatmap rejection p99 > 10 → WARN
verify latency Time series p99 > 2× baseline → WARN
decaps_reject_total rate Rate graph Spike > 10× baseline → anomaly alert
sig_verify_reject_total rate Rate graph Spike > 10× baseline → anomaly alert
rng_error_total Counter Any increment → PAGE
key_file_permission_warn Counter Any increment → WARN

Alerting Philosophy

  • P0 (page immediately): any KAT failure; any RNG error; constant-time test regression in CI.
  • P1 (ticket + on-call notify): rejection loop p99 spike; malformed-input rejection rate spike (possible active adversary probing).
  • P2 (weekly review): permission warnings; latency regression >20%.
  • No alert on normal implicit rejections; they are expected and measured.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement Window Burn-Rate Alert
KAT correctness 100% pass Every CI run Any fail
Keygen latency (ML-KEM-768) p99 < 500 µs 7-day rolling p99 > 1 ms for 1 h
Sign latency (ML-DSA-65) p99 < 2 ms 7-day rolling p99 > 5 ms for 1 h
Decaps implicit rejection indistinguishability Timing CV < 1% Phase 14 bench Fail → P0
Malformed-input rejection (no panic) 100% Fuzz CI Any panic

Incident Response

  1. Detect: CI KAT failure or metric alert fires.
  2. Triage: identify failing parameter set and operation from kat_fail_total labels and log vector_id.
  3. Isolate: reproduce with pinned ACVP vector; bisect to phase using per-module KAT subsets.
  4. Mitigate: pin prior dep versions; disable affected param set if partial fix available.
  5. Fix: correct implementation; re-run full KAT + fuzz corpus.
  6. Post-mortem: document root cause, invariant violated, and phase that should have caught it.

Runbooks

Runbook Trigger Steps Summary
RB-01: KAT Regression pqc_kat_fail_total increments Identify vector, bisect dep change, diff with reference impl, fix, re-run
RB-02: Timing Leak Suspected Timing CV > threshold in CI or report Run dudect; review Phase 12 audit log; replace branch; re-bench
RB-03: Secret in Memory Sanitizer finds secret in freed mem Locate missing zeroize; patch Drop; rotate affected keys
RB-04: Malformed Input Panic Fuzz discovers panic Identify input; fix parse/bounds; add to regression corpus
RB-05: RNG Failure in Production pqc_rng_error_total > 0 Check OS entropy pool; restart after entropy available; alert ops
RB-06: Dep Supply-Chain Alert Cargo.lock hash mismatch in CI Freeze prior lock; audit diff; engage crate maintainer
RB-07: High Signing Rejection Rate Rejection histogram p99 spike Confirm param constants; check for RNG degradation; profile
RB-08: Key File Exposed Permission warn fires chmod 0600; audit access logs; rotate key pair

Backup / Artifact Management

  • KAT ACVP vector files stored in pqc-kat/vectors/; committed to repo with SHA-256 checksum file.
  • Fuzz corpus directories (pqc-kat/fuzz/corpus/) tracked in version control.
  • Cargo.lock committed; reproducible builds via cargo build --locked.
  • Secret key files: operator responsibility; no automated backup; rotation is the recovery path.
  • CI artifacts: test reports, timing bench results, sanitizer logs retained 90 days.

Deployment Topology

  • Library use: pqc-kem and pqc-sign as Rust library crates; no daemon, no network listener; zero deployment surface beyond the binary embedding them.
  • CLI use (pqc-cli): single stateless binary; no persistent state; safe to run multiple instances in parallel.
  • Canary: embed in a test harness with sampled key operations; compare KAT pass/fail before full rollout.
  • Rolling upgrade: replace binary atomically; no migration needed (stateless).
  • Rollback: revert to prior pinned binary; re-run KAT to confirm.
  • Migration: key files are self-describing (parameter set encoded in header); no in-place migration needed on binary update.

Compatibility & Versioning

Scope

Compatibility applies to three surfaces: (A) public Rust API (types and function signatures), (B) serialized binary formats (KemPublicKey, KemSecretKey, Ciphertext, SharedSecret, SignPublicKey, SignSecretKey, Signature), and (C) CLI command-line interface.

Semantic Versioning Policy

Version Bump Trigger
PATCH (0.x.Y) Bug fix with identical wire output; KAT still passes; no API change
MINOR (0.X.0) New parameter set added; new optional API method; backward-compatible serialization extension
MAJOR (X.0.0) Breaking API rename; wire format change for existing param set; removal of a type or function

Pre-1.0 (0.x): MINOR bumps may include breaking API changes with deprecation notice in changelog. Post-1.0: strict SemVer.

Wire / Binary Format Compatibility

Type Format Source Versioning Change Policy
KemPublicKey FIPS 203 sec. 5 byte layout (rho ‖ t_encode) Normative; tied to FIPS revision Change only on FIPS errata; bump MAJOR
KemSecretKey FIPS 203 sec. 5 (dk = (z,H(ek),ek,sk)) Normative Same as above
Ciphertext FIPS 203 sec. 5 (Compress(u)‖Compress(v)) Normative Same as above
SharedSecret 32 raw bytes Normative Fixed; never changes
SignPublicKey FIPS 204 sec. 5 (rho ‖ t1_encode) Normative Change only on FIPS errata; bump MAJOR
SignSecretKey FIPS 204 sec. 5 (rho‖K‖tr‖s1‖s2‖t0) Normative Same as above
Signature FIPS 204 sec. 5 (c~‖z_encode‖h_encode) Normative Same as above

All wire formats are FIPS-normative. Any change to a wire format is a MAJOR version bump. No application-layer framing or versioning header is added on top of FIPS byte strings; framing is the caller's responsibility.

API Stability

Surface Stability Level Notes
pqc-kem public API (keygen, encaps, decaps) Stable post-1.0 Signature changes → MAJOR
pqc-sign public API (keygen, sign, verify) Stable post-1.0 Same
KemParams, SignParams constants Stable; new values additive Adding a new param set → MINOR
pqc-core error types Stable; new variants additive New error variant → MINOR (non-exhaustive enum)
pqc-ntt, pqc-poly internal APIs Unstable; internal crates No cross-crate stability guarantee until 1.0
pqc-kat harness API Internal; may change Not part of public stable surface
pqc-cli subcommand interface Stable post-1.0 (flags, exit codes) Removals → MAJOR; additions → MINOR

Type / Schema Evolution

  • KemParams and SignParams are non-exhaustive structs; callers must not pattern-match exhaustively — new fields may be added in MINOR releases.
  • Error enums (PqcError) are #[non_exhaustive]; new variants are MINOR bumps.
  • Domain enum is closed (Normal, Ntt only); new variants would be MAJOR.
  • Poly.coeffs layout ([i32; 256]) is fixed by R_q definition; never changes.
  • PolyVec and PolyMat dimensions are parameterized by K/L from KemParams/SignParams; adding a new parameter set does not break existing code.

Migration Guarantees

Scenario Guarantee
Upgrading pqc-kem PATCH release Existing serialized public keys and ciphertexts remain valid; no re-keying required
Upgrading pqc-kem MINOR release Existing keys valid; newly added param set available without migration
Upgrading pqc-kem MAJOR release Wire format may change; re-keying required; migration guide in CHANGELOG
FIPS errata that changes byte layout MAJOR release; old and new formats explicitly non-interoperable; no silent migration
Downgrade from MINOR Supported; new param-set keys fail to parse (unknown param set error), others unaffected

Deprecation Policy

  • A symbol deprecated in version X.Y.0 is removed no earlier than version (X+1).0.0.
  • Deprecated items carry #[deprecated(since = "X.Y.0", note = "use Foo instead")].
  • Deprecation notices are listed in CHANGELOG under a Deprecated heading.
  • No deprecated item is removed in a MINOR or PATCH release.

Rolling-Upgrade Guarantees

  • pqc-kem and pqc-sign are stateless libraries; rolling upgrade of the binary embedding them requires no coordination.
  • Encapsulated ciphertexts generated by version N are decapsulable by version N+1 provided the FIPS wire format has not changed (i.e., no MAJOR bump between N and N+1).
  • Signatures generated by version N are verifiable by version N+1 under the same condition.
  • Mixed-version deployments (producer on N, consumer on N+1) are safe across PATCH and MINOR boundaries.

Interoperability with External Implementations

  • Byte-exact FIPS compliance (I1) guarantees interoperability with any correct ML-KEM or ML-DSA implementation (liboqs, BouncyCastle, reference pq-crystals C implementations).
  • No proprietary extensions to the FIPS byte formats; no version header prepended.
  • CLI input/output uses raw FIPS-encoded bytes; framing (PEM, DER wrapping) is out of scope and the caller's responsibility.
  • ACVP KAT pass is the interoperability conformance gate; passing all ACVP vectors is a release prerequisite.

Testing & Verification Strategy

Unit Testing

Target Method Pass Criterion
Zq add/sub/mul exhaustive over small ranges; property: result in [0,q) I8
Montgomery/Barrett round-trip ∀ x ∈ [0,q²): montgomery(barrett(x)) == x mod q I8
Poly add/sub component-wise: coeffs match manual exact
Schoolbook multiply sparse polys with known products (constant, linear) exact
Domain tag enforcement compile-fail test: mul(NTT, Normal) must not compile P6
CBD sampler output distribution: mean=0, variance=eta/2 over 10^5 draws statistical
Rejection sampler output range [0,q), no off-by-one at q-1 I8
Bit-packing encode/decode roundtrip over random coefficients at each d ∈ {1..12} I9
Compress/Decompress Decompress(Compress(x)) - x
Dilithium pack/unpack t1/t0/s1/s2/z/h roundtrip for all parameter sets I9
SHA3-256/512 NIST CAVS test vectors byte-exact
SHAKE128/256 NIST XOF test vectors; streaming multi-squeeze byte-exact
G/H/PRF/XOF wrapper FIPS 203/204 named-function vectors I1

Integration Testing

Scenario Method
ML-KEM-512/768/1024 encaps→decaps round-trip fresh keygen per run; assert SharedSecret equality
ML-DSA-44/65/87 sign→verify fresh keygen; verify returns true
Decaps tampered ciphertext flip 1 byte; assert SharedSecret != honest SharedSecret (implicit rejection)
Verify tampered signature/message assert verify == false, no panic
Tampered public-key format assert Err(MalformedKey), no panic
Multi-parameter sweep all 6 parameter sets (3 KEM + 3 DSA) in one CI job
API surface: pqc-cli keygen/encaps/decaps/sign/verify end-to-end CLI invocations with file I/O

Differential Testing (Reference Implementation)

  • NTT vs. schoolbook: ntt_mul(a,b) == schoolbook_mul(a,b) for 10^4 random Poly pairs per q value — I5.
  • pq-crystals/kyber reference (C): cross-compile reference under #[cfg(test)]; run same KAT seeds through both, assert byte-identical keys/ciphertexts/secrets.
  • pq-crystals/dilithium reference (C): same pattern; assert byte-identical keys/signatures.
  • RustCrypto ml-kem crate: encaps output compared on identical seed inputs.
  • Run differential suite on every PR touching pqc-ntt, pqc-kem, pqc-sign.

Property-Based Testing (proptest / quickcheck)

Property Generator Invariant
inv_ntt(fwd_ntt(p)) == p random Poly with coeffs in [0,q) I5
Encoding roundtrip random PolyVec, d ∈ {1..12} I9
Schoolbook mul commutativity random pairs (a,b) algebraic
CBD output in [-eta, eta] random 32-byte seeds, eta ∈ {2,3} spec
Compress error bound random coeffs in [0,q) at each (du,dv) FIPS bound
Zq reduction idempotent random i32 values including negative I8
FO implicit rejection: decaps(tampered) ∈ {0,1}^256, not a panic random 1-bit flips in ciphertext I4, I10

Fuzzing

  • Engine: cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer) with arbitrary crate for structured inputs.
  • Fuzz targets (each a separate binary under fuzz/fuzz_targets/):
Target Input Assertion
fuzz_decaps arbitrary ciphertext bytes no panic; no branch on secret; returns SharedSecret or rejection key
fuzz_verify arbitrary signature + message bytes no panic; returns Ok(bool)
fuzz_decode_poly arbitrary byte slice, random d no panic; error or valid Poly
fuzz_decode_keys_kem arbitrary byte slice no panic; error or valid KemPublicKey
fuzz_decode_keys_sign arbitrary byte slice no panic; error or valid SignPublicKey
fuzz_ntt arbitrary Poly no panic; roundtrip identity
  • Fuzz corpus seeded from KAT byte strings plus bit-flips; minimized corpus committed to fuzz/corpus/.
  • CI runs fuzz targets for 60 s each; long soak (24 h) triggered on release branches.

Deterministic-Replay / Determinism Testing

  • All randomness enters through an explicit rng: &mut impl RngCore parameter; pure functions take seeds.
  • KAT harness (pqc-kat) feeds a deterministic DRBG (counter-mode seed from ACVP) to keygen/sign; same seed → same output byte-for-byte.
  • Replay test: serialize RNG state before keygen; restore and re-run; assert identical outputs.
  • CI enforces cargo test -- --nocapture never prints differing outputs across two consecutive runs of the same binary.

Conformance / KAT Validation

  • FIPS 203 ACVP: all KAT vectors for ML-KEM-512, 768, 1024 — keygen, encaps, decaps.
  • FIPS 204 ACVP: all KAT vectors for ML-DSA-44, 65, 87 — keygen, sign, verify.
  • KAT runner in pqc-kat: reads NIST JSON test-vector files; calls public API; asserts hex equality.
  • Any byte mismatch fails CI with a diff of expected vs. actual hex.
  • KAT vectors tracked in kat/ directory; versioned; never auto-generated.

Fault Injection and Chaos

Fault Method Expected outcome
Zero-length ciphertext pass empty slice to decaps Err(MalformedCiphertext)
Wrong-length public key truncate by 1 byte Err(MalformedKey)
All-zero ciphertext pass 0^n to decaps implicit rejection SharedSecret, not panic
CBD seed length 0 empty byte stream to sampler Err(InsufficientEntropy)
Overflow coefficient in encoded poly inject 0xFF...FF Err(CoefficientOutOfRange)

Soak / Long-Running Tests

  • 10^6 keygen+encaps+decaps cycles per parameter set under Valgrind (checks for use-after-free, leaks in non-secret paths).
  • 10^6 keygen+sign+verify cycles per DSA parameter set; count abort-loop iterations; assert mean < spec bound.
  • Soak tests gated behind #[cfg(feature = "soak")]; run nightly in CI.

Performance Regression

  • Criterion benchmarks (see Performance Engineering) record baseline JSON artifacts in CI.
  • Any PR that degrades NTT throughput >5%, keygen >10%, or encaps/decaps >10% relative to the committed baseline is flagged as a regression and blocks merge.
  • Threshold table tracked in bench/baselines/.

Compatibility Testing

  • cargo test on stable, beta, nightly Rust toolchains (matrix in CI).
  • Target triples: x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu, aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu, x86_64-apple-darwin, wasm32-unknown-unknown (no-std mode).
  • #![no_std] compliance checked: build pqc-core, pqc-poly, pqc-ntt, pqc-hash, pqc-ct with --no-default-features; must compile without std.

Build & Release Engineering

Toolchain and Workspace Layout

Concern Choice
Toolchain pin rust-toolchain.toml pins exact stable version; nightly only for fuzzing target
Workspace Cargo workspace; each pqc-* crate is a member
MSRV declared in each Cargo.toml; cargo-msrv verifies on every PR
no_std crates pqc-core, pqc-poly, pqc-ntt, pqc-hash, pqc-ct, pqc-sample, pqc-encode — #![no_std]; alloc allowed only where noted

Reproducible Builds

  • Cargo.lock committed; --locked flag used in all CI and release builds.
  • RUSTFLAGS and --release profile locked in config.toml; no opt-level surprises.
  • Hermetic Docker build image: pinned rust:<version>-alpine digest in Dockerfile.build.
  • SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set in release Makefile; binary timestamps deterministic.
  • Two independent builders produce artifacts; SHA-256 digests compared before publishing.

CI/CD Topology

  • PR pushed
    • ├── lint (cargo fmt --check, cargo clippy -- -D warnings)
    • ├── test matrix (stable / beta / nightly × linux-x86_64 / aarch64 / macos)
    • ├── KAT conformance (cargo test -p pqc-kat --release)
    • ├── no_std build check
    • ├── fuzz (60 s per target, libFuzzer)
    • ├── MSRV check
    • ├── dependency audit (cargo-audit)
    • └── bench regression gate (compare to baseline artifact)
  • main merge
    • ├── all PR checks re-run
    • ├── SBOM generation (cargo-cyclonedx)
    • └── nightly soak + long fuzz (24 h, triggered on schedule)
  • release tag
    • ├── reproducible release build (2 × hermetic builders, digest comparison)
    • ├── artifact signing (sigstore cosign; SLSA level 3 provenance)
    • ├── publish to crates.io (cargo publish --locked)
    • └── GitHub Release with attached SBOM + provenance attestation

Version Stamping

  • CARGO_PKG_VERSION embedded at compile time via build.rs; also embeds Git SHA (git describe --always --dirty).
  • pqc_core::version() returns (crate_version, git_sha, toolchain_version) tuple.
  • Semver: each pqc-* crate versioned independently; public-API changes trigger minor/major bumps.

Dependency Auditing and Supply-Chain

Tool Trigger Action on failure
cargo-audit every PR + nightly block merge; file issue
cargo-deny every PR policy: no duplicate versions of security-sensitive crates
cargo vet release builds supply-chain vetting with audits committed to supply-chain/
Dependabot / Renovate weekly auto-PR with version bumps
cargo tree --duplicates release gate alert on duplicates

Artifact Signing and SBOM

  • Release binaries signed with cosign (keyless, Sigstore transparency log).
  • SLSA provenance attestation (level 3) generated by the CI pipeline and attached to the GitHub Release.
  • SBOM in CycloneDX JSON format (cargo-cyclonedx) published alongside each release.
  • SBOMs archived in sbom/ in the repo under a release/<tag> namespace.

Release Channels

Channel Branch Cadence Audience
stable main + version tag as ready production users
nightly main HEAD daily build integrators / early testing
pre-release rc/* before each minor beta testers

Performance Engineering

Targets (from Constraints / Phase 14)

ID Operation Parameter Set Target (single core, release build, x86_64 AVX2)
M1 ML-KEM keygen 768 ≤ 50 µs
M2 ML-KEM encaps 768 ≤ 60 µs
M3 ML-KEM decaps 768 ≤ 65 µs
M4 ML-DSA keygen 65 ≤ 100 µs
M5 ML-DSA sign 65 ≤ 400 µs (amortized over rejection loops)
M6 ML-DSA verify 65 ≤ 150 µs
M7 NTT (single poly) n=256, q=3329 ≤ 1 µs
M8 NTT (single poly) n=256, q=8380417 ≤ 1.5 µs
M9 SHAKE128 throughput — ≥ 1 GB/s (hardware Keccak path)
M10 ct_eq_bytes (32-byte shared secret) — ≤ 50 ns (Capacity Model item 10)
M11 KAT suite full run (all 6 parameter sets) — ≤ 60 s (System Constraints §4.1)

Targets are informational aspirations; FIPS byte-exact correctness and constant-time (I1, I2) take precedence. M3 and M10 are the two numbers the Capacity Model (item 5, item 10) derives from first principles; M1–M9 chain from the §4.1 throughput/latency rows.

Benchmarking Strategy

  • Harness: Criterion.rs; one benchmark binary per crate.
  • Benchmark IDs mirror phase structure: ntt/forward/kyber, kem/encaps/mlkem768, sign/sign/mldsa65, etc.
  • Warm-up: Criterion default (3 s); measurement: 100 iterations minimum, auto-scaled.
  • Parameterized over all parameter sets; aggregated throughput reported as ops/s.
  • Baseline JSON artifacts committed to bench/baselines/<toolchain>/<arch>/; regression check on CI (>5% NTT, >10% KEM/DSA).

Profiling Methodology

Phase Tool Target
Hot-path identification perf record + perf report (Linux) / Instruments (macOS) NTT butterfly loop, SHAKE permutation
Flamegraph cargo-flamegraph → flamegraph.svg keygen, sign (including abort loop)
Instruction-level IACA / llvm-mca on NTT inner loop verify butterfly throughput
Cache behavior perf stat -e cache-misses,cache-references twiddle-table access pattern
Branch prediction perf stat -e branch-misses confirm constant-time paths have no secret-dependent branches

Tracing Methodology

  • tracing crate with tracing-subscriber; spans at operation boundaries: keygen, encaps, decaps, sign, verify.
  • Span attributes: parameter-set name, elapsed µs — never secret values (P10).
  • Rejection-loop counter emitted as a tracing::event! histogram (P10); used to tune abort probabilities, not secret-dependent data.
  • Release builds default to RUST_LOG=warn; perf tracing enabled via RUST_LOG=pqc=trace.

Allocator Analysis

  • pqc-core through pqc-encode are no_std; stack-allocated only (no heap alloc in crypto hot path).
  • pqc-kem and pqc-sign may use stack-allocated buffers for intermediate PolyVec/PolyMat (stack frame audit in Phase 12/14).
  • cargo-heaptrack or dhat used in pqc-cli and pqc-kat to confirm no unexpected heap churn in crypto operations.
  • Zeroization (zeroize crate, P8) must not be elided by the optimizer; use ZeroizeOnDrop derive.

Cache Analysis

  • Twiddle-factor table (zeta array, 256 × i32 = 1 KB for ML-KEM NTT) fits in L1 cache; keep table const, cache-line-aligned.
  • ML-DSA twiddle table (256 × i32 = 1 KB for q=8380417) similarly.
  • PolyMat for ML-KEM-1024 (4×4 × 256 × i32 = 16 KB) fits in L1 on most microarchitectures; validate with perf.
  • Avoid pointer-chasing in hot loops: PolyVec uses contiguous arrays, not Vec<Poly>.

Flamegraph Workflow

1. cargo flamegraph --bin pqc-cli -- kem encaps --kat mlkem768
2. Open flamegraph.svg; identify hottest frames (expected: NTT butterflies, Keccak-f)
3. If NTT not dominant: investigate sampler or encoding overhead
4. Annotate known-hot frames in FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md's Performance Engineering section; track in bench/notes/

Side-Channel Timing Measurements

  • dudect / ct-verif methodology: measure execution time for two input classes (random secret vs. fixed secret); compute t-statistic; assert |t| < 4.5 (no leakage detected).
  • Apply to: decaps (FO comparison path), sign (rejection loop — aggregate count only, not individual), ct_eq, ct_select.
  • Record results in ct-audit/ directory; re-run on each toolchain update.

Memory Profiling

  • Stack depth measured with cargo-call-stack for each crypto operation; must not exceed 64 KB on embedded targets.
  • Heap usage in pqc-cli measured with heaptrack; expected: O(key_size + ciphertext_size) peak.
  • Zeroize coverage: valgrind --tool=memcheck --track-origins=yes confirms no use of freed secret memory.

Scalability Analysis

System Character

Post-quantum KEM and DSA are stateless, embarrassingly parallel, single-operation workloads. There is no persistent server, no shared mutable state, no registry, and no coordination between operations. Scaling pressure is therefore fundamentally different from a distributed system.

Dominant Scaling Pressure

Pressure Source Notes
Parameter-set growth additional NIST parameter levels (e.g., future ML-KEM-1024 variants) additive code paths; tested independently
KAT vector set size ACVP JSON files grow with each NIST update O(n) test time; parallelize with --test-threads
Rejection-loop variance Dilithium signing may loop 1–7 times; throughput non-deterministic bounded by FIPS spec; not a scalability concern
Twiddle-table size fixed at 256 entries for n=256; does not grow constant
Key/ciphertext byte sizes ML-KEM-1024 pk=1568 B, ct=1568 B; ML-DSA-87 sig=4627 B fits in L1/L2; no streaming required

Parallelism Model

  • Per-operation: no internal parallelism required; single-threaded crypto path is sufficient for target latencies.
  • Multi-user throughput: operations are independent; scale by running N concurrent threads each executing one operation — no shared state, no lock contention.
  • Batch keygen / encaps: for high-throughput use cases (e.g., TLS mass deployment), parallelize with rayon; each operation is fully independent.
  • pqc-kat: KAT vectors for different parameter sets are independent; cargo test parallelizes by default.

Horizontal Scaling

  • Library is stateless; horizontal scaling is trivially achieved by running more processes or threads.
  • No coordinator, no shared cache, no distributed state.
  • Throughput scales linearly with core count up to memory-bandwidth saturation (twiddle table read-mostly → fits in shared L2/L3 across cores).

Vertical Scaling Ceilings

Resource Ceiling Mitigation
Instruction throughput NTT butterfly loop: ~4–8 cycles/butterfly on OoO core SIMD (AVX2/NEON) vectorization of butterfly in Phase 14
Memory bandwidth twiddle table (1 KB) fits in L1; not a ceiling at current n=256 n=256 is fixed by spec; no growth
Stack depth bounded by polynomial size (~4 KB per PolyMat for ML-KEM-768) constant; does not grow with scale
SHAKE throughput Keccak-f 1600 is the hash bottleneck for key expansion hw-accelerated SHA3 (x86 SHA3 ext, ARMv8.2 crypto)

Bottleneck Map

keygen (ML-KEM-768)
  40% — SHAKE128 XOF (matrix A expansion from rho)
  35% — NTT forward/inverse (A*s, A*e products)
  15% — CBD sampling (noise vectors)
  10% — encoding/compression

sign (ML-DSA-65, 1 iteration)
  45% — NTT (A*y, A*z matrix-vector products)
  30% — SHAKE256 (challenge hashing, PRF)
  15% — norm-check and hint computation
  10% — encoding (z, h packing)

Sharding and Partitioning

  • Not applicable: no persistent data, no registry, no distributed state.
  • If used as a microservice, partition by parameter set (one worker pool per level) to improve cache locality on twiddle tables.

Metadata and Registry Growth

  • Parameter sets are compile-time constants (6 total: 3 KEM + 3 DSA); no runtime registry growth.
  • KAT vector files grow with NIST ACVP updates; test runtime grows linearly — parallelize with --test-threads N.

Scheduler and Storage Growth

  • No scheduler. Operations complete in O(n log n) time (NTT-dominated); n=256 is fixed by the standard.
  • No persistent storage beyond key files (O(key_size)); no database, no log accumulation in the library itself.
  • pqc-kat vector storage: ~100 MB total for all FIPS 203/204 ACVP JSON; grows slowly with spec revisions.

Future-Proofing

Potential standard evolution Impact Mitigation
FIPS 205 (SPHINCS+ / SLH-DSA) new crate pqc-slh; no changes to existing crates modular crate architecture
New ML-KEM / ML-DSA parameter sets add variant to KemParams/SignParams enum; one new KAT file Phase 1 extensibility
n > 256 NTT (hypothetical) twiddle table + polynomial arrays scale with n factored NTT; parameterize n
AVX-512 / SVE SIMD optional feature flag; C FFI to asm; no ABI changes Phase 14 SIMD path behind feature gate

Mental Models & First Principles

These models compress expertise into load-bearing intuitions. Each names the insight, what it predicts, the bug class that emerges without it, and the resolving section.

MM-01. Lattice problems are hard because small things are short but look random. The insight: a short vector s drawn from a small distribution (|s_i| ≤ η) satisfies As+e = b, but b is computationally indistinguishable from uniform — this is Module-LWE hardness (Regev 2005, Pa-1). What it predicts: no polynomial-time algorithm can recover s from (A, b) without knowing the noise distribution, even given many samples. Bug class without it: choosing η too large (η ≥ 6) makes the noise distribution distinguishable from uniform, collapsing IND-CCA2 to distinguishing; choosing η = 0 (no noise) reduces to a linear-algebra problem solvable by Gaussian elimination. Resolving §: §6 P8 (parameter provenance), Phase 1 §1.2 (KemParams/SignParams constants from FIPS 203/204).

MM-02. The Fujisaki-Okamoto transform plugs a single-use-key hole. The insight: CPA-secure encryption leaks nothing about a single fresh message, but an adversary who can submit many ciphertexts and see decryption results (CCA2 oracle) can recover the key. FO patches this by making the encapsulated key deterministic in the message (re-derive r from m via G), so a tampered ciphertext decapsulates to the same PRF output regardless of tampering (Hofheinz-Hövelmanns-Kiltz 2017, Pa-5). What it predicts: a decapsulation routine that ever returns an error (rather than PRF(z, c)) is CCA2-broken — the error is an oracle. Bug class without it: any early-exit error path in decaps (malformed ciphertext before the FO path) leaks one bit per query; 256 queries suffice to determine the shared secret. Resolving §: §6 P3 (FO CCA2 mandate), Phase 7 §7.2 (decaps design).

MM-03. Rejection sampling makes the response distribution secret-independent. The insight: Lyubashevsky's Fiat-Shamir with aborts (2009, Pa-7) masks s1 with a uniform vector y. The response z = y + c·s1 is distributed over a hypercube only if the norm bound is satisfied — conditioned on acceptance, z's distribution is independent of s1. What it predicts: the acceptance probability per iteration is approximately (1 − 2β/γ1)^256L; for ML-DSA-65 this is about 80 %. Bug class without it: if rejection is removed, z leaks c·s1 directly; if y is reused across iterations, successive z_i values share a bias that allows lattice-reduction recovery of s1. Resolving §: §6 P4 (rejection-sampling principle), Phase 8 §8.2 (signing loop).

MM-04. Phantom-type domain tags prevent a class of NTT correctness bugs at zero runtime cost. The insight: a polynomial in Normal domain and a polynomial in NTT domain are the same bit pattern — the ring isomorphism is invisible to the type system unless you tag it. Adding Domain { Normal, Ntt } as a field (or const generic tag) lets the compiler reject poly_add(a_ntt, b_normal) at compile time. What it predicts: without the tag, pointwise multiplication of two Normal polynomials silently produces garbage that passes coefficient-range checks (coefficients are in [0, q)) but is semantically wrong — the error surfaces only in the final KAT or in rare bit-flip patterns. Bug class without it: incorrect-domain composition; silent KAT failures; non-deterministic test failures depending on optimizer's polynomial reordering. Resolving §: ADR-10, Phase 2 §2.2 (Domain tag design), Phase 4 §4.2 (NTT/polynomial integration).

MM-05. A constant-time comparison is a conditional move, not a conditional branch. The insight: (a == b) in C or Rust compiles to sete / setne followed by a branch at the call site if the result is used in an if. subtle::ConstantTimeEq returns a Choice that is #[repr(transparent)] over u8; the black_box barrier prevents LLVM from widening it to bool and folding it into a branch. The selected value is produced by arithmetic: (mask & a) | (!mask & b) emitting cmov or equivalent. What it predicts: LLVM at -O2 will collapse -(a ^ b) >> 63 to cmov on x86-64 and to csel on AArch64 — but only if the value is never widened to bool. Bug class without it: timing side-channel in FO decapsulation comparison; 1-bit leak per query; 256 queries determine the shared secret. Resolving §: §6 P1 (constant-time mandate), Phase 12 §12.2 (pqc-ct design), Pa-14 (Bernstein-Yang barrier pattern).

MM-06. Every hash function in the ML-KEM/ML-DSA protocol is a domain-separated sponge. The insight: SHA3-256, SHA3-512, SHAKE128, SHAKE256 are all instantiations of Keccak-f[1600] with different rate/capacity splits and different padding suffixes: 0x06 (SHA-3) vs 0x1F (SHAKE). ML-KEM assigns G = SHA3-512, H = SHA3-256, PRF = SHAKE256, XOF = SHAKE128; ML-DSA assigns H = SHAKE256. Each usage is domain-separated by the function identity, not by an extra prefix. What it predicts: substituting SHAKE128 for SHA3-256 (H) produces the same output length but breaks IND-CCA2 because the 128-bit capacity is insufficient for the 256-bit binding security claim. Bug class without it: silent security-level downgrade; byte-identical outputs for different security levels when the same seed is used; KAT mismatch if padding byte is wrong. Resolving §: §6 P6 (hash-based domain separation), Phase 5 §5.2 (pqc-hash XOF interface), Pa-8 (FIPS 202 Keccak spec).

MM-07. Montgomery reduction keeps coefficients normalized without an explicit modular division. The insight: Montgomery multiplication computes (a · b · R^{-1}) mod q where R = 2^k for a chosen k (R = 2^16 for q = 3329, R = 2^23 for q = 8380417). The trick: replace the expensive div by q with a mul by q^{-1} mod R followed by a right-shift — all operations are integer multiply and shift. The output is in (-q, q), not [0, q), so one conditional add (constant-time) reduces to canonical form. What it predicts: after 256 pointwise NTT multiplications, coefficients drift into the range (-q · N, q · N) unless Barrett or Montgomery normalization is applied at each butterfly layer. Bug class without it: coefficient overflow in i32 midway through a 128-layer NTT; silent wrap-around producing wrong coefficients that still pass range checks but fail KATs. Resolving §: §6 P5 (reduction principle), Phase 3 §3.2 (Montgomery/Barrett implementation), Pa-3 (original Kyber spec, §1.1).

MM-08. Zeroization requires a volatile write or a compiler barrier; otherwise it is a no-op. The insight: a compiler with knowledge that a variable is never read after memset(p, 0, n) is free to delete the memset as dead code. The zeroize crate prevents this by writing through a volatile raw pointer or by calling compiler_fence(SeqCst) after the write, forcing the store to survive optimization. What it predicts: without the barrier, LTO (Link-Time Optimization) erases the zeroize call in release builds, leaving secret key material in stack/heap memory until overwritten by the next allocation — exploitable via cold-boot or heap-dump attacks. Bug class without it: secret key material persists in memory after drop(signing_key); exploitable in WASM (linear memory dump), TEE (attestation snapshot), or after panic unwind. Resolving §: §6 P8 (secret erasure principle), Phase 12 §12.2 (ZeroizeOnDrop barrier), ADR-05.

Comparative Architecture Table

This blueprint's design choices compared against four real, publicly documented PQC library systems on eight load-bearing dimensions.

Dimension This Blueprint (pqc workspace) liboqs / OQS (C, Open Quantum Safe) BoringSSL / Google PQC (C, production TLS) Cloudflare CIRCL (Go) NIST Reference Impls (C, pq-crystals)
Language Rust 2021, no_std core C99, platform-dependent C with assembly (AArch64, x86-64 SIMD) Go 1.21+ C99
CT guarantees subtle crate barriers; dudect statistical test Partial; CT not guaranteed across all platforms; MSVC branch risk on cmov High; hand-audited assembly CT paths; CI timing tests Limited; Go compiler provides weak CT guarantees; no formal review Not CT on all platforms; reference purpose only
FIPS alignment Targets full FIPS 203/204 ACVP compliance; KAT vectors checked-in FIPS 203/204 implementations present; alignment ongoing with spec finalization FIPS 140-3 validated module boundary; ML-KEM integrated as of BoringSSL 2024-Q4 Kyber round-3 / draft ML-KEM; FIPS 203 alignment partial as of 2024 Normative FIPS 203/204 reference; Appendix A KAT seeds are the ground truth
Dependency surface subtle + zeroize (+ optional sha3 crate); minimal liboqs links OpenSSL for hash; broader attack surface Links boringssl internals; well-audited but opaque Standard library + golang.org/x/crypto; medium surface No external dependencies; self-contained C
Embedded / no_std 7 of 11 crates no_std + no_alloc; 2 crates no_std + alloc Not no_std; requires OS and libc; not suitable for embedded HSM Not no_std; requires OS Not no_std Not no_std
NTT strategy Cooley-Tukey forward, Gentleman-Sande inverse; static Montgomery zeta table Platform-adaptive; AVX2 paths for x86-64; ARM NEON paths Highly optimized: AVX2 + AArch64 assembly; lazy reduction in inner loop Schoolbook + NTT (Go, portable) Canonical reference NTT; correctness-first, no SIMD
KAT / validation FIPS 203/204 ACVP JSON; CI-blocked; pqc-kat crate KAT vectors embedded; external ACVP submission supported Internal KAT only; not published; validated via FIPS 140-3 lab test Custom test vectors; not FIPS ACVP aligned NIST-authored; KAT seeds are normative per Appendix A
Multi-algorithm scope ML-KEM + ML-DSA only; SLH-DSA deferred ML-KEM, ML-DSA, Falcon, SPHINCS+, McEliece, and others ML-KEM (NIST round 4); Dilithium partially; no Falcon ML-KEM, Kyber, Dilithium, NTRU ML-KEM + ML-DSA reference only

liboqs (Open Quantum Safe) — one paragraph. liboqs is the broadest-coverage open-source PQC library: it implements not only ML-KEM/ML-DSA but also Falcon, McEliece, NTRU, and several candidates from other NIST competitions (see open-quantum-safe.org). It links OpenSSL for SHA-3, which keeps its dependency surface manageable but prevents no_std use and introduces OpenSSL's ABI into the trust model. liboqs does not guarantee constant-time operation across all compilers and platforms — the cmov-vs-branch risk on MSVC is documented but unmitigated. It wins in scenarios where algorithm agility across many PQC families matters more than CT rigor or embedded deployment: a research server testing Falcon vs ML-DSA performance tradeoffs, or a TLS proxy that must negotiate across six PQC families depending on the peer's capability.

BoringSSL / Google PQC deployment — one paragraph. Google began shipping ML-KEM in Chrome TLS handshakes (as X25519MLKEM768) starting Chrome 131 (2024-Q4), using BoringSSL's hand-audited ML-KEM implementation with AArch64 and x86-64 assembly paths. The CT guarantees are high: assembly paths are reviewed against the LLVM IR and the generated machine code is tested with timing regression tests in CI. The tradeoff is opacity: BoringSSL is not designed for external embedding or no_std use; it is tightly coupled to Chromium's build system. It wins when the deployment target is TLS in a high-traffic production environment where a well-audited, FIPS-validated module boundary is contractually required and embedded use is off the table.

Cloudflare CIRCL — one paragraph. CIRCL (Cloudflare Interoperable Reusable Cryptographic Library, github.com/cloudflare/circl) is a Go library containing ML-KEM (draft and final), Kyber round-3, Dilithium, and several other primitives. Go's garbage collector and lack of volatile write guarantee make constant-time properties harder to verify; CIRCL documents this limitation. The NTT is implemented in portable Go, not assembly. It wins in a Go microservice context where the team does not have C or Rust expertise, speed-to-deployment is paramount, and the threat model does not include a local timing adversary (cloud-hosted service with no same-host attacker).

NIST C reference implementations — one paragraph. The pq-crystals reference implementations (pq-crystals/kyber, pq-crystals/dilithium) are the normative byte-level truth: FIPS 203 Appendix A KAT seeds are designed to reproduce their output exactly. They are correctness-first, not performance-first, and explicitly not constant-time on all platforms. They have no external dependencies and compile with any C99 compiler. They win as the oracle for KAT verification (every other implementation must match them), for reading to understand algorithm structure before performance optimization, and as the reference for byte-format decisions in FIPS 203/204 Appendix B.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

All inputs taken from §4 System Constraints unless otherwise noted. Each number is derived step-by-step.

1. ML-KEM-768 public key size. Formula: |ek| = k·ρ·256·d_bits/8 + 32 bytes seed where k=3, d=12 bits (t vector), ρ=1 byte seed appended. Calculation: 3 polynomials × 256 coefficients × 12 bits / 8 = 3 × 384 = 1152 bytes, plus 32-byte seed = 1184 bytes (matches FIPS 203 sec. 2.4 Table 1).

2. ML-KEM-768 ciphertext size. Formula: |c| = k·du·256/8 + dv·256/8 where du=10, dv=4. Calculation: 3 × 10 × 256 / 8 = 960 bytes (u vector) + 4 × 256 / 8 = 128 bytes (v scalar) = 1088 bytes (matches FIPS 203 Table 1).

3. ML-DSA-65 signature size. Formula: |sig| = λ/4 + l·γ1·256·(1+ceil(log2(2γ1)))/8 + ceil(k·ω/8) + ω bytes. Simplified per FIPS 204 sec. 2.5: |sig| = 32 + l·(2γ1 bits) · 256/8 + (ω + k) bytes. Calculation: 32 + 5 × (2^20 × 256 / 8) + (55 + 6) bytes = 32 + 5 × 32768 + 61 = 32 + 163840 + 61 = 3309 bytes (matches FIPS 204 Table 1: 3309 bytes).

4. TLS 1.3 hybrid handshake bandwidth (X25519MLKEM768). Components: X25519 key share (32 B) + ML-KEM-768 ciphertext (1088 B) + ML-KEM-768 public key (1184 B). One-shot server→client data: ciphertext only (client already sent pk): 1088 + 32 = 1120 bytes. Total handshake key-exchange overhead vs. classical X25519: 1120 − 64 = +1056 bytes per handshake. At 10 000 TLS handshakes/s: additional bandwidth = 1056 × 10 000 = 10.56 MB/s additional key-exchange traffic.

5. Per-operation latency budget (ML-KEM-768 decaps, ARM Cortex-A72, single-core). From §4 System Constraints: target ≤ 500 µs decaps on Cortex-A72. NTT dominates at 45 % of decaps time → NTT budget: 0.45 × 500 µs = 225 µs. ML-KEM-768 decaps requires 2 forward NTTs and 1 inverse NTT for the re-encryption. Per-NTT budget: 225 µs / 3 = 75 µs → NTT must run in ≤ 75 µs on a 1.5 GHz Cortex-A72. At 1.5 GHz with ~8 cycles per butterfly: 128 NTT layers × 128 butterflies/layer × 8 cycles = 131072 cycles = 87 µs — within 20 % margin, requires single-cycle multiply (hardware multiplier present on A72).

6. Signing rejection-loop expected iterations (ML-DSA-65). Acceptance probability per iteration: p ≈ (1 − 2β/γ1)^{256L} where β=196, γ1=2^19=524288, L=5. 2β/γ1 = 392/524288 ≈ 0.000748; (1 − 0.000748)^1280 ≈ e^{−0.000748×1280} = e^{−0.958} ≈ 0.384. Expected iterations E[N] = 1/p ≈ 2.6 iterations per signature (FIPS 204 sec. 3.3 states expected ~2.5 iterations for ML-DSA-65 — consistent).

7. Memory footprint per ML-KEM-768 decapsulation (stack, no alloc).
